A H 3 ​ ( G , 𝕋 ) H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}}) -valued index of symmetry protected topological phases with on-site finite group symmetry for two-dimensional quantum spin systems
Yoshiko Ogata

Abstract
We consider SPT-phases with on-site finite group G G symmetry β \beta for two-dimensional quantum spin systems. We show that they have H 3 ​ ( G , 𝕋 ) H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}}) -valued invariant.



1
Introduction

The notion of symmetry protected topological (SPT) phases was introduced by Gu and Wen [GW].
It is defined as follows:
we consider the set of all Hamiltonians with some symmetry,
which have a unique gapped ground state in the bulk, and can be smoothly deformed into
a common trivial gapped Hamiltonian without closing the gap.
We say
two such Hamiltonians are equivalent, if they can be smoothly deformed into
each other, without breaking the symmetry.
We call an equivalence class of this classification, a
symmetry protected topological (SPT) phase.
Based on tensor network or TQFT analysis,
[
CGLW
]
,
[
MGSC
]

it is conjectured that
SPT phases with on-site finite group
G
symmetry for
\nu
-dimensional quantum spin systems
have a
H^{\nu+1}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
-valued invariant.
We proved their conjecture affirmatively in
[
O1
]
for
\nu=1
.
In this paper, We
show that the conjecture is also true for
\nu=2
.

We start by summarizing standard setup of
2
-dimensional quantum spin systems on the two dimensional lattice
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

[
BR1
,
BR2
]
. We will use freely the basic notation in section
A
.
Throughout this paper, we fix some
2\leq d\in\mathbb{N}
.
We denote the algebra of
d\times d
matrices by
\mathop{\mathrm{M}}\nolimits_{d}
.

For each subset
\Gamma
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
we denote the set of all finite subsets in
\Gamma
by
{\mathfrak{S}}_{\Gamma}
.
We introduce the Euclidean metric on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, inherited from
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.
We denote by
\mathrm{d}(S_{1},S_{2})
the distance between
S_{1},S_{2}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
.
For a subset
\Gamma
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
and
r\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
,

\hat{\Gamma}{(r)}
denotes the all points in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
whose distance from
\Gamma
is less than or equal to
r
.
We also set
\Gamma(r):=\hat{\Gamma}{(r)}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
.
When we take a complement of
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
it means
\Gamma^{c}:={\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\setminus\Gamma
.
For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we denote
[-n,n]^{2}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
by
\Lambda_{n}
.

For each
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\{z\}}
be an isomorphic copy of
\mathop{\mathrm{M}}\nolimits_{d}
, and for any finite subset
\Lambda\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we set
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}=\bigotimes_{z\in\Lambda}{\mathcal{A}}_{\{z\}}
.
For finite
\Lambda
, the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
can be regarded as the set of all bounded operators acting on
the Hilbert space
\bigotimes_{z\in\Lambda}{{\mathbb{C}}}^{d}
.
We use this identification freely.
If
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}}
is naturally embedded in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}
by tensoring its elements with the identity.
For an infinite subset
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}

is given as the inductive limit of the algebras
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
with
\Lambda\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{\Gamma}
.
We call
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
the quantum spin system on
\Gamma
.
For simplicity we denote the two dimensional quantum spin system
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

by
{\mathcal{A}}
.
We also set
{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}:=\bigcup_{\Lambda\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
.
For a subset
\Gamma_{1}
of
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
can be regarded as a subalgebra of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
.
With this identification, for
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
, we occasionally use the same symbol

A
to denote
A\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus\Gamma_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
.
Similarly, an automorphism
\gamma
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
can be naturally regarded as an automorphism

\gamma\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus\Gamma_{1}}}
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
.
We use this identification freely and with a slight abuse of notation
we occasionally denote
\gamma\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\setminus\Gamma_{1}}}
by
\gamma
.
Similarly, for disjoint
\Gamma_{-},\Gamma_{+}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
and
\alpha_{\pm}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\pm}
,
we occasionally write
\alpha_{-}\otimes\alpha_{+}
to denote

\left(\alpha_{-}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{-}^{c}}\right)\left(\alpha_{+}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\Gamma_{+}^{c}}\right)
,
under the above identification.

Throughout this paper we fix a finite group
G
and its unitary representation
U
on
{\mathbb{C}}^{d}
.
Let
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
be a non-empty subset.
For each
g\in G
, there exists a unique automorphism
\beta^{\Gamma}
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}

such that

\displaystyle\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\left(A\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bigotimes_{I}U(g)\right)\left(A\right),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{I},\quad g\in G,

(1.1)

for any finite subset
I
of
\Gamma
.
We call the group homomorphism
\beta^{\Gamma}:G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
,
the on-site action of
G

on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
given by
U
.
For simplicity, we denote
\beta^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}_{g}
by
\beta_{g}
.

A mathematical model of a quantum spin system is fully specified by its interaction
\Phi
.
A uniformly bounded interaction on
{\mathcal{A}}

is a map
\Phi:{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}
such that

\displaystyle\Phi(X)=\Phi(X)^{*}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X},\quad X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},

(1.2)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\left\|\Phi(X)\right\|<\infty.

(1.3)

It is of finite range with interaction length less than or equal to
R\in\mathbb{N}
if

\Phi(X)=0
for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

whose diameter is larger than
R
.
An on-site interaction, i.e., an interaction with

\Phi(X)=0
unless
X
consists of a single point, is said to be trivial.
An interaction
\Phi
is
\beta
-invariant if
\beta_{g}(\Phi(X))=\Phi(X)

for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
For a uniformly bounded and finite range interaction
\Phi
and
\Lambda\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

define the local Hamiltonian

\displaystyle\left(H_{\Phi}\right)_{\Lambda}:=\sum_{X\subset\Lambda}\Phi(X),

(1.4)

and denote the dynamics

\displaystyle\tau^{(\Lambda)\Phi}_{t}(A):=e^{it\left(H_{\Phi}\right)_{\Lambda}}Ae^{-it\left(H_{\Phi}\right)_{\Lambda}},\quad t\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

(1.5)

By the uniform boundedness and finite rangeness of
\Phi
,
for each
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
, the following limit exists

\displaystyle\lim_{\Lambda\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\nu}}\tau^{(\Lambda),\Phi}_{t}\left(A\right)=:\tau^{\Phi}_{t}\left(A\right),\quad t\in{\mathbb{R}},

(1.6)

and defines the dynamics
\tau^{\Phi}
on
{\mathcal{A}}
.
(See
[
BR2
]
.)
For a uniformly bounded and finite range interaction
\Phi
,
a state
\varphi
on
{\mathcal{A}}
is called a
\tau^{\Phi}
-ground
state
if the inequality

-i\,\varphi(A^{*}{\delta_{\Phi}}(A))\geq 0

holds
for any element
A
in the domain
{\mathcal{D}}({\delta_{\Phi}})
of the generator
{\delta_{\Phi}}
.
Let
\varphi
be a
\tau^{\Phi}
-ground state, with a GNS triple
({\mathcal{H}}_{\varphi},\pi_{\varphi},\Omega_{\varphi})
.
Then there exists a unique positive operator
H_{\varphi,\Phi}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\varphi}
such that

e^{itH_{\varphi,\Phi}}\pi_{\varphi}(A)\Omega_{\varphi}=\pi_{\varphi}(\tau^{t}_{\Phi}(A))\Omega_{\varphi}
,
for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
We call this
H_{\varphi,\Phi}
the bulk Hamiltonian associated with
\varphi
.

Definition 1.1
.

We say that an interaction
\Phi
has a unique gapped ground state if
(i) the
\tau^{\Phi}
-ground state, which we denote as
\omega_{\Phi}
, is unique, and
(ii) there exists a
\gamma>0
such that

\sigma(H_{\omega_{\Phi},\Phi})\setminus\{0\}\subset[\gamma,\infty)
, where
\sigma(H_{\omega_{\Phi},\Phi})
is the spectrum of
H_{\omega_{\Phi},\Phi}
.
We denote by
{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
the set of all uniformly bounded finite range
interactions, with unique gapped ground state.
We denote by
{\mathcal{P}}_{UG\beta}
the set of all uniformly bounded finite range

\beta
-invariant
interactions, with unique gapped ground state.

In this paper we consider a classification problem of a subset of
{\mathcal{P}}_{UG\beta}
, models with short range entanglement.
To describe the models with short range entanglement, we need
to explain the classification problem of unique gapped ground state phases, without symmetry.
For
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we denote by
\Pi_{\Gamma}:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
the conditional expectation with respect to the trace state.
Let
f:(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
be a continuous decreasing function
with
\lim_{t\to\infty}f(t)=0
.
For each
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
, let

\displaystyle\left\|A\right\|_{f}:=\left\|A\right\|+\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\frac{\left\|A-\Pi_{\Lambda_{N}}(A)\right\|}{f(N)}\right).

(1.7)

We denote by
{\mathcal{D}}_{f}
the set of all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
such that

\left\|A\right\|_{f}<\infty
.

The classification of unique gapped ground state phases
{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
without symmetry is the following.

Definition 1.2
.

Two interactions
\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
are equivalent if there is
a path of interactions

\Phi:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}

satisfying the following:

1.

\Phi(0)=\Phi_{0}
and
\Phi(1)=\Phi_{1}
.

2.

For each
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
, the map

[0,1]\ni s\to\Phi(X;s)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
is
C^{1}
.
We denote by
\dot{\Phi}(X;s)

the corresponding derivatives.
The interaction obtained by differentiation is denoted by
\dot{\Phi}(s)
, for each
s\in[0,1]
.

3.

There is a number
R\in\mathbb{N}

such that
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\nolimits{X}\geq R
imply
\Phi(X;s)=0
, for all
s\in[0,1]
.

4.

Interactions are bounded as follows

\displaystyle C_{b}^{\Phi}:=\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\sup_{X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\left(\left\|\Phi\left(X;s\right)\right\|+|X|\left\|\dot{\Phi}\left(X;s\right)\right\|\right)<\infty.

(1.8)

5.

Setting

\displaystyle b(\varepsilon):=\sup_{Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\sup_{s,s_{0}\in[0,1],0<|s-s_{0}|<\varepsilon}\left\|\frac{\Phi(Z;s)-\Phi(Z;s_{0})}{s-s_{0}}-\dot{\Phi}(Z;s_{0})\right\|

(1.9)

for each
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}b(\varepsilon)=0
.

6.

There exists a
\gamma>0
such that

\sigma(H_{\omega_{\Phi(s)},\Phi(s)})\setminus\{0\}\subset[\gamma,\infty)
for
all
s\in[0,1]
, where
\sigma(H_{\omega_{\Phi(s)},\Phi(s)})
is the spectrum of
H_{\omega_{\Phi(s)},\Phi(s)}
.

7.

There exists an
0<\eta<1
satisfying the following:
Set
\zeta(t):=e^{-t^{\eta}}
.
Then for each
A\in D_{\zeta}
,

\omega_{\Phi(s)}(A)
is differentiable with respect to
s
, and there is a constant

C_{\zeta}
such that:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{ds}\omega_{\Phi(s)}(A)\right|\leq C_{\zeta}\left\|A\right\|_{\zeta},

(1.10)

for any
A\in D_{\zeta}
.(Recall (
1.7
)).

We write
\Phi_{0}\sim\Phi_{1}
if

\Phi_{0}
and
\Phi_{1}
are equivalent.
If
\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG\beta}
and
if we can take the path in
{\mathcal{P}}_{UG\beta}
, i.e.,
so that
\beta_{g}\left(\Phi(X;s)\right)=\Phi(X;s)
,
g\in G
for all
s\in[0,1]
,
then we say
\Phi_{0}
and
\Phi_{1}
are
\beta
-equivalent
and write
\Phi_{0}\sim_{\beta}\Phi_{1}
.

The object we classify in this paper is the following:

Definition 1.3
.

Fix a trivial interaction

\Phi_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
.
We denote by
{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
the set of all

\Phi\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG\beta}

such that
\Phi\sim\Phi_{0}
.
Connected components of

{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
with respect to

\sim_{\beta}
are the
symmetry protected topological (SPT)-phases.

Because we have
\Phi_{0}\sim\tilde{\Phi}_{0}
for any trivial
\Phi_{0},\tilde{\Phi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
, the set

{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
does not depend on the choice of
\Phi_{0}
.

The main result of this paper is as follows.

Theorem 1.4
.

There is a
H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
-valued index on
{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
, which is an invariant of the classification

\sim_{\beta}
of
{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
.

This paper is organized as follows.
In section

2
, we define the
H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
-valued index
for a class of states which are created from a fixed product state
via “factorizable” automorphisms, and satisfying some additional condition.
This additional condition is the existence of the set of
automorphisms 1.which do not move the state, and 2. are almost like

\beta
-action restricted to the upper half-plane except for some one-dimensional
perturbation.
In section

3
, we show that the existence of such set of
automorphisms are guaranteed in suitable situation.
Furthermore, in section

4
, we show the stability of the index, i.e.,
suitably
\beta
-invariant automorphism does not
change this index.
Finally in section

5
, we show our main Theorem
Theorem
1.4
, showing that in our setting of Theorem
1.4
,
all the conditions required in section

2
,
3
,
4

are satisfied.
Although the index is defined in terms of GNS representations,
in some good situation,
we can calculate it without going through
GNS representation. This is shown in section

6
.

The present result and the main idea of the proof were announced publicly on 15 December 2020 at IAMP One World Mathematical Physics Seminar (see you-tube video)
[
O2
]
.
It was also presented in the international meeting
Theoretical studies of topological phases of matter

on 17 December 2020, and
Current Developments in Mathematics 4th January 2021 via zoom with a lecture note.

[
O2
]
.

2
The
H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
-valued index in
2
-dimensional systems

In this section, we associate an
H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
-index for some class of states.
It will turn out later that this class includes SPT phases.

2.1
Definitions and the setting

For
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
, a cone
C_{\theta}
is defined by

\displaystyle C_{\theta}:=\left\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\mid|y|\leq\tan\theta\cdot|x|\right\}.

(2.1)

For
0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
, we use a notation

{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}:=C_{\theta_{2}}\setminus C_{\theta_{1}}
and

{\mathcal{C}}_{[0,\theta_{1}]}:=C_{\theta_{1}}
.
Left, right, upper, lower half planes are denoted by
H_{L}
,
H_{R}
,

H_{U}
,
H_{D}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle H_{L}:=\left\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\mid x\leq-1\right\},\quad H_{R}:=\left\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\mid 0\leq x\right\},

(2.2)

\displaystyle H_{U}:=\left\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\mid 0\leq y\right\},\quad H_{D}:=\left\{(x,y)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\mid y\leq-1\right\}.

(2.3)

We use a notation
\beta_{g}:=\beta_{g}^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
\beta_{g}^{U}:=\beta_{g}^{H_{U}}
,

\beta_{g}^{RU}:=\beta_{g}^{H_{R}\cap H_{U}}
,
\beta_{g}^{LU}:=\beta_{g}^{H_{L}\cap H_{U}}
.

For each subset
S
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we set

\displaystyle S_{\sigma}:=S\cap H_{\sigma},\quad S_{\zeta}:=S\cap H_{\zeta},\quad S_{\sigma\zeta}:=S\cap H_{\sigma}\cap H_{\zeta}\quad\sigma=L,R,\quad\zeta=U,D.

(2.4)

We ocationally write
{\mathcal{A}}_{S,\sigma}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{S,\zeta}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{S,\sigma,\zeta}

to denote
{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{\sigma}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{\zeta}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{S_{\sigma\zeta}}
.
For an automorphism
\alpha
on
{\mathcal{A}}
and
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
we denote by
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
a set of all triples
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)

with

\displaystyle\alpha_{L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}\right),\quad\alpha_{R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}\right),\quad\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}}\right)

(2.5)

decomposing
\alpha
as

\displaystyle\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Theta.

(2.6)

For
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{(\theta)}_{\alpha}
,
we set

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}:=\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}.

(2.7)

The class of automorphisms
which allow such decompositions for any directions are denoted by

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right):=\left\{\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\mid\;{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset\;\;\text{for all}\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}\right\}.

(2.8)

Furthermore, for each

\displaystyle 0<\theta_{0.8}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{1.2}<\theta_{1.8}<\theta_{2}<\theta_{2.2}<\theta_{2.8}<\theta_{3}<\theta_{3.2}<\frac{\pi}{2},

(2.9)

we consider decompositions of
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
such that

\displaystyle\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)

(2.10)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha_{X}:=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R,\zeta=D,U}\alpha_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\quad\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}:=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma},\quad\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}:=\bigotimes_{\zeta=D,U}\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}\\
&\alpha_{X,\sigma,\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{X,\sigma,\zeta}}\right),\quad\alpha_{X,\sigma}:=\bigotimes_{\zeta=U,D}\alpha_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\quad\alpha_{X,\zeta}:=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\alpha_{X,\sigma,\zeta}\\
&\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{[0,\theta_{0}],\sigma}}\right),\quad\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}}\right),\end{split}

(2.11)

for

\displaystyle X=(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}],\quad\sigma=L,R,\quad\zeta=D,U.

(2.12)

The class of automorphisms on
{\mathcal{A}}

which allow such decompositions for any directions

\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1},\theta_{1.2}
,
\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2},\theta_{2.2},\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3},\theta_{3.2}

(satisfying (
2.9
)) is denoted by
\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Note that
\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
The set of all
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
with
each of

\alpha_{I}
in the decompositions required to commute with
\beta_{g}^{U}
,
g\in G
,
is denoted by
\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}):=\left\{\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\middle|\begin{gathered}\text{for any}\;\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1},\theta_{1.2},\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2},\theta_{2.2},\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3},\theta_{3.2}\quad\text{satisfying (\ref {thetas1})}\\
\text{there is a decomposition }(\ref {sqaut}),(\ref {sqaut2}),(\ref {sqaut3})\;\text{satisfying}\\
\alpha_{I}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}=\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha_{I},\quad g\in G,\\
\text{for all}\;\;I=[0,\theta_{1}],(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],\left(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}\right],(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]\end{gathered}\right\}.

We also define

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right):=\left\{\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\middle|\begin{gathered}\text{for any}\;\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;\;\text{there exist}\;\;\alpha_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{\sigma}}}\right),\sigma=L,R\\
\text{such that}\;\;\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)\end{gathered}\right\}.

In section
5
, we will see that
quasi-local automorphisms corresponding to paths in symmetric gapped phases
belong to the following set:

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right):=\left\{\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)\middle|\begin{gathered}\text{there are}\;\;\gamma_{H}\in\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}),\gamma_{C}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\\
\text{such that}\;\;\gamma=\gamma_{C}\circ\gamma_{H}\end{gathered}\right\}.

We fix a reference state
\omega_{0}
as follows.
We fix a unit vector
{\xi_{x}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{d}
and let
\rho_{\xi_{x}}
be the vector state on

\mathop{\mathrm{M}}\nolimits_{d}
given by
{\xi_{x}}
, for each
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
.
Then our reference state
\omega_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle\omega_{0}:=\bigotimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\rho_{\xi_{x}}.

(2.21)

Throughout this section this
\omega_{0}
is fixed.
Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{0},\pi_{0},\Omega_{0})
be a GNS triple of
\omega_{0}
.
Because of the product structure of
\omega_{0}
,
it is decomposed as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{0}={\mathcal{H}}_{L}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{R},\quad\pi_{0}=\pi_{L}\otimes\pi_{R},\quad\Omega_{0}=\Omega_{L}\otimes\Omega_{R},

(2.22)

where
({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma},\pi_{\sigma},\Omega_{\sigma})
is a GNS triple of

\omega_{\sigma}:=\omega_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}}
for
\sigma=L,R
.
As
\omega_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}}
is pure,
\pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible.
What we consider in this section is the set of states
created via elements in
\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
from our reference state
\omega_{0}
:

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits:=\left\{\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\mid\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\right\}.

(2.23)

Because any pure product states can be transformed to each other via
an automorphism of product form
\tilde{\alpha}=\bigotimes_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\tilde{\alpha}_{x}

and
\tilde{\alpha}\alpha
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
for any
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
,

\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
does not depend on the choice of
\omega_{0}
.
For each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
, we set

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega):=\left\{\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})\mid\omega=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\right\}.

(2.24)

By the definition of
\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
,
\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
is not empty.

For
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}

and a set of automorphisms

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})_{g\in G}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({{\mathcal{A}}})
,
we introduce a set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})):=\left\{(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})_{g\in G,\sigma=L,R}\middle|\begin{gathered}\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{\sigma}}\right),\\
\tilde{\beta}_{g}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U},\\
\text{for all}\;g\in G,\;\sigma=L,R\end{gathered}\right\}.

In a word, it is a set of decompositions of
\tilde{\beta}_{g}\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}

into tensor of
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{L}}\right)
,

\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{R}}\right)
modulo inner automorphisms.
For
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})_{g\in G,\sigma=L,R}\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
,
we set

\displaystyle\eta_{g}:=\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R},\quad g\in G.

(2.28)

The following set of automorphisms is the key ingredient for the definition of our index.
For
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
and
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right):=\left\{(\tilde{\beta}_{g})_{g\in G}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)^{\times G}\middle|\begin{gathered}\omega\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}=\omega\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in G,\\
\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))\neq\emptyset\end{gathered}\right\}.

We also set

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega\right):=\cup_{0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right).

(2.31)

In this section we associate some third cohomology
h(\omega)

for each
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
.

2.2
Derivation of elements in
Z^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})

In this subsection, we derive
3
-cocycles out of
\omega
,
\alpha
,
\theta
,

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})
,
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)
.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right)
,
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
.
Then

(i)

There are unitaries
W_{g}
,
g\in G
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1},\quad g\in G

(2.32)

with notation (
2.7
), (
2.28
).

(ii)

There exist a unitary
u_{\sigma}(g,h)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}
, for each
\sigma=L,R
,
g,h\in G
,
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\eta_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1},

(2.33)

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{L}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(2.34)

Furthermore,
u_{\sigma}(g,h)

commutes with any element of

\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}\right)_{\sigma}}\right)
.

Definition 2.2
.

For
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right)
,
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})_{g\in G,\sigma=L,R}\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
,
we denote by

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right)

(2.35)

the set of
\left((W_{g})_{g\in G},(u_{\sigma}(g,h))_{g,h\in G,\sigma=L,R}\right)
with

W_{g}\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{H}}_{0}\right)
and
u_{\sigma}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}\right)

satisfying

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1},\;g\in G,\quad\text{and}

(2.36)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\eta_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1},\quad g,h\in G,\sigma=L,R.

(2.37)

(Here we used notation (
2.7
) and (
2.28
).)
By Lemma
2.1
, it is non-empty.

For a GNS triple
({\mathcal{H}}_{0},\pi_{0}\circ\alpha,\Omega_{0})
of
\omega=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha

there are unitaries
\tilde{W}_{g}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{W}_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g},\quad g\in G

(2.38)

because
\omega\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}=\omega
.

Because
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})_{g\in G,\sigma=L,R}\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
,
and
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
,
there are unitaries
v_{g},V\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{g}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}\right)\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U},\quad\alpha=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits V\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta.

(2.39)

Substituting these, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{W}_{g}\pi_{0}(V)\right)\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\tilde{\beta}_{g}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(v_{g})\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha(v_{g})\right)\pi_{0}(V)\right)\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}.

(2.40)

Therefore, setting
W_{g}:=\pi_{0}(V)^{*}\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha(v_{g}^{*})\right)\tilde{W}_{g}\pi_{0}(V)\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{0})
,
we obtain (
2.32
).

Using this (
2.32
), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{*}\right)\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh}^{-1}\Theta^{-1}\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(2.41)

Note that because conjugation by
\beta_{g}^{U}
does not change the support of
automorphisms,
\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh}^{-1}
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}}\right)
.
On the other hand,
\Theta
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}}\right)
.
Therefore, they commute and we obtain

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{*}\right)\pi_{0}=(\ref {wwwgh})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\eta_{gh}^{-1}\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\eta_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}

(2.42)

From this and the irreducibility of
\pi_{R}
, we see that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{*}\right)

gives rise to a
*
-isomorphism
\tau
on
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R})
.It is implemented by
some unitary
u_{R}(g,h)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{R}
by the Wigner theorem and
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{R}(A)\right)=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tau\left(\pi_{R}(A)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}(A)\right)\\
&=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{R}^{-1}(A),\end{split}

(2.43)

for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}
.
Hence we obtain (
2.33
) for
\sigma=R
.

To see that
u_{R}(g,h)
belongs to
\left(\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}\right)_{R}}\right)\right)^{\prime}
,
let
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}\right)_{R}}
.
Then because
\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}

belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{R}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R}(g,h)\right)\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}(A)\right)=\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}\alpha_{R}(A)=\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}(A).

(2.44)

This proves that
u_{R}(g,h)
belongs to
\left(\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}\right)_{R}}\right)\right)^{\prime}
.
Analogous statement for
u_{L}(g,h)
can be shown exactly the same way.
The last statement (
2.34
) of (ii) is trivial from (
2.33
).

\square

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right)
,
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
.
Let
\left((W_{g}),(u_{R}(g,h))\right)
be an element of
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right)
.

Then the followings hold.

(i)

For any
g,h,k\in G
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\alpha_{R}\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}\right)\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}\right).

(2.45)

(ii)

For any
g,h\in G
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(u_{L}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}\right),

(2.46)

on
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{0})
.

(iii)

For any
g,h,k\in G
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)\in{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R}).

(2.47)

(iv)

For any
g,h,k,f\in G
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}\right)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right).

(2.48)

We use the notation (
2.7
), (
2.28
).

(i)
Substituting (
2.36
) (
2.37
), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{R}^{-1}\right)\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\Theta\circ\left(\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(2.49)

Because
\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}
belongs to

\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{R}}\right)
,
it commutes with
\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}}\right)
.
Hence we obtain

\displaystyle(\ref {2ichi})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\left(\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(2.50)

Again, the term in the round braket in the last line is localized at
\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{R}
, and it commutes with
\Theta
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\eta_{k}^{R}\left(\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{hk}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{R}^{-1}\right)

(2.51)

(ii)
Again by (
2.36
) and (
2.37
), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(u_{L}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{gh}\beta_{gh}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}\right)^{-1}\circ\eta_{gh}\beta_{gh}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\eta_{h}\beta_{h}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}\right)\circ\pi_{0}.

(2.52)

Here, for the second equality, we again used the commutativity of
\eta
s and
\Theta
, due to their
disjoint support.
Because
\pi_{0}
is irreducible, we obtain (
2.46
).

(iii)
For any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\left(A\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}}\right)=\Theta^{-1}\circ\left(\alpha_{L}^{-1}(A)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}}\right)\in\Theta^{-1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}}\right)\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}\cup\left(C_{\theta}^{c}\right)_{R}},

(2.53)

because
\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}}\right)
.
Therefore,
\eta_{g}^{R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{R}})
acts trivially on it and
we have

\displaystyle\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{g}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\left(A\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}}\right)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}\cup\left(C_{\theta}^{c}\right)_{R}}.

(2.54)

As
\Theta
preserves
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}\cup\left(C_{\theta}^{c}\right)_{R}}
,

\displaystyle\Theta\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{g}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\left(A\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}}\right)

(2.55)

also belongs to
{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}\cup\left(C_{\theta}^{c}\right)_{R}}
.
As a result,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{L}(A)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}}\right)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{g}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\left(A\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{R}}}\right)

(2.56)

belongs to

\pi_{L}({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}})\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}^{c}\right)_{R}}\right)
,
hence commutes with
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)
.
Hence
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{g})\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)

commutes with any elements in
\pi_{L}({\mathcal{A}}_{L})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}}
.
Because
\pi_{L}
is irreducible,

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{g})\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)

belongs to
{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R})
.

(iv)
By (iii),
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{gh}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)

belongs to
{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{R})
.
Therefore, from (ii), we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}W_{h}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(u_{L}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right),

(2.57)

obtaining (iv).

\square

With this preparation we may obtain some element of
Z^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})

from
((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h)))
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
,
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right)
,

(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
,

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
.
Let
\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)
be an element of
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right)
.
Then there is a
c_{R}\in Z^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})

such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)=c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)\right),

(2.58)

for all
g,h,k\in G
.

Definition 2.5
.

We denote this
3
-cocycle
c_{R}
in the Lemma by

\displaystyle c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right)

(2.59)

and its cohomology class by

\displaystyle h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right):=\left[c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right)\right]_{H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})}.

(2.60)

First we prove that there is a number
c_{R}(g,h,k)\in{\mathbb{T}}

satisfying (
2.58
).
From (
2.37
),we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)\right)\pi_{0}=\pi_{L}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)\left(\eta_{k}^{R}\beta_{k}^{RU}\right)\left(\eta_{ghk}^{R}\beta_{ghk}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}.

(2.61)

On the other hand, using (i) of Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)\right)\right)\pi_{0}

(2.62)

is also equal to the right hand side of (
2.61
).
Because
\pi_{0}
is irreducible, this means that there is a number
c_{R}(g,h,k)\in{\mathbb{T}}

satisfying (
2.58
).

Now let us check that this
c_{R}
is a
3
-cocycle.
For any
g,h,k,f\in G
, by repeated use of (
2.58
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)u_{R}(ghk,f)=\left[\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)\right]\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(ghk,f)\right)

(2.63)

\displaystyle=\left(c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(ghk,f)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\right)\cdot\left[\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)u_{R}(ghk,f)\right]

\displaystyle=\left(c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\right)\cdot\left(c_{R}(g,hk,f)\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(hk,f)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hkf)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)\left(W_{g}\left[\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)u_{R}(hk,f)\right]W_{g}^{*}\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hkf)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)W_{g}\left(W_{h}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)W_{h}^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,kf)\right)\right)W_{g}^{*}\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hkf)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)\cdot W_{g}W_{h}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)W_{h}^{*}W_{g}^{*}\cdot\left[\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,kf)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hkf)\right]

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)\overline{c(g,h,kf)}\cdot\left\{W_{g}W_{h}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)W_{h}^{*}W_{g}^{*}\right)\}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,kf)\right).

(2.64)

Here, (and below) we apply (
2.58
) for terms in
\left[\cdot\right]
to get the succeeding equality.
Applying (iv) of Lemma
2.3
to the
\{\cdot\}
part of (
2.64
),
we obtain

\displaystyle(\ref {temp})

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)\overline{c(g,h,kf)}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)W_{gh}\right)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,kf)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)\overline{c(g,h,kf)}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)\right)\left[W_{gh}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(k,f)\right)W_{gh}^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(gh,kf)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)\overline{c(g,h,kf)}\overline{c_{R}(gh,k,f)}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)u_{R}(ghk,f)\right).

(2.65)

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle c_{R}(g,h,k)c_{R}(g,hk,f)c_{R}(h,k,f)\overline{c(g,h,kf)}\overline{c_{R}(gh,k,f)}=1,\quad\text{for all}\quad g,h,k,f\in G.

(2.66)

This means
c_{R}\in Z^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
.

\square

2.3
The
H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
-valued index

From the previous subsection, we remark the following fact.

Lemma 2.6
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
,
there are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega),\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\theta\right),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})),\;(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha},\\
\left((W_{g}),(u_{R}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).\end{split}

(2.67)

Because
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
, there is some
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta)\neq\emptyset
,
and hence
(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta)
and

(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
exist.
Because

\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
, by definition there exists some
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)

and by the definition of
\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
,
there is some
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathfrak{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha}
.
The existence of
\left((W_{g}),(u_{R}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right)
is given by Lemma
2.1
.

\square

By Lemma
2.4
, for

\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
,
for each choice of (
2.67
),
we can associate some element of

H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
:

\displaystyle h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right).

(2.68)

In this subsection, we show that the third cohomology class does not depend on
the choice of (
2.67
):

Theorem 2.7
.

For any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
,

h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right)

is independent of the choice of

\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right).

Definition 2.8
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
.
We denote the third cohomology given in Theorem
2.7
by

h(\omega):=h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right).

First we show the independence from
\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)
.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega),\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right),\;(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta},

(2.69)

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right),\left((\tilde{W}_{g}),(\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(2.70)

Then
we have

\displaystyle h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right)=h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((\tilde{W}_{g}),(\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right).

(2.71)

Definition 2.10
.

From this lemma and because there is always

\left((W_{g}),(u_{R}(g,h))\right)

in
\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right)
by
Lemma
2.1
, we may
define

\displaystyle h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right):=h^{(1)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right)

(2.72)

for any

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega),\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right),\;(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta},

(2.73)

independent of the choice of
\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)
.

Because

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{W}_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0},

(2.74)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{R}=\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{R}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{R}

(2.75)

and
\pi_{0}
,
\pi_{R}
are irreducible,
there are
b(g),a(g,h)\in{\mathbb{T}}
,
g,h\in G
such that

\displaystyle W_{g}=b(g)\tilde{W}_{g},\quad\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)=a(g,h)u_{R}(g,h).

(2.76)

Set

\displaystyle c_{R}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{R}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((\tilde{W}_{g}),(\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right).

(2.77)

Then from the definition of these values and (
2.76
), we have

\displaystyle a(g,h)a(gh,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)\right)=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)\tilde{u}_{R}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{R}(g,h,k)\left(\tilde{W}_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}(h,k)\right)\tilde{W}_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}(g,hk)\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{R}(g,h,k)a(h,k)a(g,hk)\left(W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{c}_{R}(g,h,k)a(h,k)a(g,hk)\overline{c_{R}(g,h,k)}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,h)u_{R}(gh,k)\right).

(2.78)

Hence we have
\tilde{c}_{R}(g,h,k)=c_{R}(g,h,k)\overline{a(h,k)a(g,hk)}a(g,h)a(gh,k)
, and
we get
[c_{R}]_{H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})}=[\tilde{c}_{R}]_{H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})}
, proving the claim.

\square

Next we show the independence from
\alpha,(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)
.

Lemma 2.11
.

Let

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega),\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right),

(2.79)

\displaystyle(\alpha_{L,1},\alpha_{R,1},\Theta_{1})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha_{1}}^{\theta},\quad(\alpha_{L,2},\alpha_{R,2},\Theta_{2})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha_{2}}^{\theta}.

(2.80)

Then
we have

\displaystyle h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\alpha_{1},\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L,1},\alpha_{R,1},\Theta_{1})\right)=h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\alpha_{2},\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L,2},\alpha_{R,2},\Theta_{2})\right).

(2.81)

Definition 2.12
.

From this lemma
and because there are always

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)

and
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta}

for
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
and
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
by the definition,
we may
define

\displaystyle h^{(3)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\right):=h^{(2)}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right)

(2.82)

for any

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;,\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right),

(2.83)

independent of the choice of
\alpha,(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)
.

By Lemma
2.1
, there are

\displaystyle\left((W_{g,1}),(u_{\sigma,1}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha_{1},\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L,1},\alpha_{R,1},\Theta_{1})\right).

(2.84)

For each
i=1,2
,
we have
\Theta_{i}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}^{c}}

and

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0,i}\circ\Theta_{i},

(2.85)

setting

\displaystyle\alpha_{0,i}:=\alpha_{L,i}\otimes\alpha_{R,i}.

(2.86)

Because
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{1}=\omega=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{2}
,
we have
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\omega_{0}
.
Therefore, there is a unitary
\tilde{V}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}

such that

\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{V}\right)\circ\pi_{0}
.
Substituting (
2.85
) to this,
we see that there is a unitary
V
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}

satisfying

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,2}\circ\Theta_{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}.

(2.87)

From this, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(VW_{g,1}V^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(VW_{g,1}\right)\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta_{1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}\\
&=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,2}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\Theta_{1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta_{1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}\\
&=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,2}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1},\end{split}

(2.88)

for all
g\in G
.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,h)\right)V^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,h)\right)\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\alpha_{R,1}\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R,1}^{-1}\right)\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,2}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\Theta_{1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,1}^{-1}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\alpha_{R,1}\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R,1}^{-1}\right)\circ\alpha_{0,1}\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,2}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\Theta_{1}^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\Theta_{1}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}

(2.89)

Now, because
\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}

is an automorphism on
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}}

and
\Theta_{2}\circ\Theta_{1}^{-1}
is an automorphism
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}^{c}}
, they commute.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,h)\right)V^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=(\ref {vuv})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0,2}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{L}}}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\alpha_{0,2}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{L}\otimes\left(\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R,2}\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{R,2}\right)^{-1}\right).

(2.90)

From this equality and the fact that
\pi_{L}
is irreducible,
we see that
V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,h)\right)V^{*}
is of the form

\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,2}(g,h)
with some unitary
u_{R,2}(g,h)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{R}
.
This
u_{R,2}(g,h)
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R,2}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{R}=\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R,2}\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{R,2}\right)^{-1}.

(2.91)

Analogously, we obtain a
unitary
u_{L,2}(g,h)
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{L}

such that

\displaystyle V\left(u_{L,1}(g,h)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}}\right)V^{*}=u_{L,2}(g,h)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}},

(2.92)

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{L,2}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{L}=\pi_{L}\circ\alpha_{L,2}\eta_{g}^{L}\beta_{g}^{LU}\eta_{h}^{L}\left(\beta_{g}^{LU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{L}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{L,2}\right)^{-1}.

(2.93)

From (
2.88
), (
2.91
), (
2.92
), we see that

\displaystyle\left(\left(VW_{g,1}V^{*}\right),\left(u_{\sigma,2}(g,h)\right)\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha_{2},\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L,2},\alpha_{R,2},\Theta)\right).

(2.94)

Set

\displaystyle c_{R,1}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha_{1},\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L,1},\alpha_{R,1},\Theta_{1}),\left((W_{g,1}),(u_{\sigma,1}(g,h))\right)\right),

\displaystyle c_{R,2}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha_{2},\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L,2},\alpha_{R,2},\Theta_{2}),\left(VW_{g,1}V^{*}\right),\left(u_{\sigma,2}(g,h)\right)\right).

(2.95)

It suffices to show that
c_{R,1}=c_{R,2}
.
This can be checked directly as follows:

\displaystyle V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,h)u_{R,1}(gh,k)\right)V^{*}=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,2}(g,h)u_{R,2}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=c_{R,2}(g,h,k)\left(VW_{g,1}V^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,2}(h,k)\right)VW_{g,1}^{*}V^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,2}(g,hk)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R,2}(g,h,k)V\left(W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(h,k)\right)W_{g,1}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,hk)\right)V^{*}

\displaystyle=c_{R,2}(g,h,k)\overline{c_{R,1}(g,h,k)}V\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R,1}(g,h)u_{R,1}(gh,k)\right)V^{*}.

(2.96)

\square

Lemma 2.13
.

Let

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right).

(2.97)

Then
we have

\displaystyle h^{(3)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\right)=h^{(3)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma})\right).

(2.98)

Definition 2.14
.

From this lemma and the definition of
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta)
,
we may
define

\displaystyle h^{(4)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right):=h^{(3)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\right)

(2.99)

for any

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta),\;(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right),

(2.100)

independent of the choice of
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})
.

There are

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)

and
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta}

for
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
by the definition.
We set
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}
and

\eta_{g}:=\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}
,

\tilde{\eta}_{g}:=\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{R}
.
By Lemma
2.1
, there is some

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(2.101)

Because
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right)
,
we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{g}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U}.

(2.102)

From this, we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{L}\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{L}\right)^{-1}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{R}\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\right)^{-1}=({\rm{inner}}),

(2.103)

hence
there are unitaries
v_{g}^{\sigma}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}^{\sigma}\right).

(2.104)

Because
\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma},\eta_{g}^{\sigma}
are
automorphisms on
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},\sigma}
,

v_{g}^{\sigma}
belongs to
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},\sigma}
.
(See Lemma
B.1
.)
Setting
v_{g}:=v_{g}^{L}\otimes v_{g}^{R}
,
we obtain

\tilde{\eta}_{g}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}\right)\circ\eta_{g}
.

Set

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{g}:=\left(\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\left(v_{g}^{L}\right)\right)\otimes\left(\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\right)\right)\right)W_{g},

(2.105)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h):=\pi_{\sigma}\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\left(v_{g}^{\sigma}\cdot\left(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\right)\left(v_{h}^{\sigma}\right)\right)\right)\cdot u_{\sigma}\left(g,h\right)\cdot\pi_{\sigma}\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\left(\left(v_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

(2.106)

for each
g,h\in G
and
\sigma=L,R
.
We claim that

\displaystyle\left(\left(\tilde{W}_{g}\right),\left(\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(2.107)

First, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\tilde{\eta}_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}\right)\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\\
&=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}\right)\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\pi_{L}\alpha_{L}\left(v_{g}^{L}\right)\right)\otimes\left(\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{W}_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}.\end{split}

(2.108)

For the first equality, we substituted
\tilde{\eta}_{g}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}\right)\circ\eta_{g}
,
and for the second equality, we used the fact that
v_{g}^{\sigma}
belongs to
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},\sigma}
,
while
\Theta
is an automorphism on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c},\sigma}
.
The last equality follows from the definition of
W_{g}
.
On the other hand,
we have

\displaystyle\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ{\tilde{\eta}}_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}{\tilde{\eta}}_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left({\tilde{\eta}}_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}^{\sigma}\right)\circ\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{h}^{\sigma}\right)\circ\eta_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left({v_{gh}^{\sigma}}^{*}\right)\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\left(\left(v_{g}^{\sigma}\right)\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\left(v_{h}^{\sigma}\right)\right)\right)\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\eta_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\left({v_{gh}^{\sigma}}^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\left(\left(v_{g}^{\sigma}\right)\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\left(v_{h}^{\sigma}\right)\right)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\pi_{\sigma}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\left({v_{gh}^{\sigma}}^{*}\right)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{\sigma},

(2.109)

for all
g,h\in G
.
For the first equality, we substituted
\tilde{\eta}_{g}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{g}\right)\circ\eta_{g}
.
The third equality is the definition of
u(g,h)
.
Hence we have proven (
2.107
).

Set

\displaystyle c_{R}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{R}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((\tilde{W}_{g}),(\tilde{u}_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\right).

(2.110)

In order to show the statement of the Lemma, it suffices to show that
c_{R}=\tilde{c}_{R}
.
Substituting the definition of
\tilde{u}_{R}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)\tilde{u}_{R}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot u_{R}\left(g,h\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{gh}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{gh}^{R}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\beta_{gh}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot u_{R}\left(gh,k\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\left[u_{R}\left(g,h\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\beta_{gh}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\right]u_{R}\left(gh,k\right)\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\left[\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R}\left(g,h\right)\right)\left(\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\beta_{gh}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\right)\cdot u_{R}\left(g,h\right)\right]u_{R}\left(gh,k\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{R}^{-1}\alpha_{R}\left(\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\beta_{gh}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cdot u_{R}\left(g,h\right)u_{R}\left(gh,k\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\cdot\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\cdot\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot u_{R}\left(g,h\right)u_{R}\left(gh,k\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

(2.111)

For the fourth equality, we used the definition of
u_{R}
.
From the above equation, applying (
2.58
) to the
[\cdot]
part below, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)\tilde{u}_{R}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\cdot\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\cdot\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\left[u_{R}\left(g,h\right)u_{R}\left(gh,k\right)\right]\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\cdot\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\cdot\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left\{W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}\right\}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)\right)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right).

(2.112)

Now from the definition of
\tilde{u}_{R}
, the
\{\cdot\}
part above becomes

\displaystyle W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{h}^{R}\cdot\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right)\right).

(2.113)

Because
v_{g}^{R}
belongs to
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},R}
and

\eta_{g}^{R}
is an automorphism on
{\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},R}

while
\Theta
is an automorphism on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta}\right)^{c}}

and
\beta_{g}^{U}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},R}\right)={\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},R}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{h}^{R}\cdot\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{h}^{R}\cdot\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(\left(v_{h}^{R}\cdot\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right)=\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right).

(2.114)

Substituting this to (
2.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle W_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(h,k)\right)W_{g}^{*}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(\left(v_{h}^{R}\cdot\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\cdot\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right).

(2.115)

Substituting this,
\{\cdot\}
part of (
2.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)\tilde{u}_{R}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\cdot\left(\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\left(v_{h}^{R}\right)\cdot\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(\left(v_{h}^{R}\cdot\eta_{h}^{R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(v_{k}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\cdot\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}(g,hk)\cdot\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\right)\right)\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\cdot\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{g}^{R}\cdot\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{hk}^{R}\right)\right)^{*}\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(g,hk\right)\cdot\right)\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)\alpha_{R}\left(\left(v_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\left(\alpha_{R}\left(v_{g}^{R}\right)\right)\right)W_{g}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(g,hk\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R}(g,h,k)\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{L}\left(\alpha_{L}\left({v_{g}^{L}}^{*}\right)\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}}\right)\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\tilde{W}_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\right\}\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(g,hk\right)\right)

Because of (iii) of Lemma
2.3
,
the
\{\cdot\}
part of the last equation is equal to

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\tilde{W}_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)
.
Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}(g,h)\tilde{u}_{R}(gh,k)=c_{R}(g,h,k)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\tilde{W}_{g}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(h,k\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\tilde{u}_{R}\left(g,hk\right)\right).

(2.117)

This proves
c_{R}=\tilde{c}_{R}
, completing the proof.

\square

Lemma 2.15
.

Let

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(1)}),(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(2)})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta).

(2.118)

Then
we have

\displaystyle h^{(4)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(1)})\right)=h^{(4)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(2)})\right).

(2.119)

Definition 2.16
.

From this lemma
we may
define

\displaystyle h^{(5)}\left(\omega,\theta\right):=h^{(4)}\left(\omega,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right)

(2.120)

for any

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2},\;\text{with}\;\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta)\neq\emptyset

(2.121)

independent of the choice of
(\tilde{\beta}_{g})
.

By the definition of
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta)
, there are

\displaystyle(\eta_{g,i}^{\sigma})_{g\in G,\sigma=L,R}\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(i)})),\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,2.

(2.122)

We set
\eta_{g,i}:=\eta_{g,i}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g,i}^{R}
, for
i=1,2
.
There are

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)

and
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta}

for
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
by the definition.
Setting
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}
, we have
\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta
.
By Lemma
2.1
, there is some

\displaystyle\left((W_{g,1}),(u_{\sigma}^{(1)}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(1)}),(\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(2.123)

Set

\displaystyle K^{\sigma}_{g}:=\eta_{g,2}^{\sigma}\circ\left(\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta},\sigma}\right),\quad\text{for}\quad\sigma=L,R,\;g\in G,\quad K_{g}:=K_{g}^{L}\otimes K_{g}^{R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}}\right).

(2.124)

We claim that there are unitaries
V_{g}^{\sigma}
,
g\in G,\sigma=L,R

on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}

such that
