\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{g}^{\sigma}\right)\circ\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ K_{g}^{\sigma}\circ\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\right)^{-1}.

(2.125)

To see this, note that

\displaystyle\omega=\omega\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(i)}=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(i)}\sim_{q.e.}\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\eta_{g,i}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g,i}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U},\quad i=1,2.

(2.126)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\eta_{g,1}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g,1}^{R}\right)\sim_{q.e.}\omega\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\sim_{q.e.}\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\eta_{g,2}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g,2}^{R}\right),

(2.127)

and then using the fact that

\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}^{c}})
and

K_{g}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}})
,

\displaystyle\omega_{0}\sim_{q.e.}\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ K_{g}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ K_{g}\circ\left(\alpha_{0}\right)^{-1}=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\omega_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}K_{g}^{\sigma}\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\right)^{-1}.

(2.128)

This implies
that
\omega_{\sigma}
and
\omega_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}K_{g}^{\sigma}\left(\alpha_{\sigma}\right)^{-1}

are quasi-equivalent.
Because
\pi_{\sigma}
is irreducible, this implies the existence of a unitary
V_{g}^{\sigma}

on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}

satisfying (
2.125
), proving the claim.

Next we claim that there are unitaries
v^{\sigma}_{g,h}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{\sigma}
, for
g,h\in G
and
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{W_{g,1}}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes v^{R}_{g,h},\quad\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{W_{g,1}}\left(V_{h}^{L}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}}\right)=v^{L}_{g,h}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}},

(2.129)

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{g}^{\sigma}v^{\sigma}_{g,h}u_{\sigma}^{(1)}(g,h)\left(V_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{*}\right)\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\eta_{g,2}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\eta_{h,2}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh,2}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1},

(2.130)

for any

and
\sigma=L,R
.
To see this,
first we calculate

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\right)\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left({\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ{\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ K_{h}^{R}\circ\left(\alpha_{R}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left({\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ{\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes K_{h}^{R}\right)\circ\Theta\circ\left({\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ{\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes K_{h}^{R}\right)\circ\left({\eta_{g,1}}\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes{\eta_{g,1}^{R}}\beta_{g}^{RU}K_{h}^{R}\left({\eta_{g,1}^{R}}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\right)\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ{\eta_{g,1}^{R}}\beta_{g}^{RU}K_{h}^{R}\left({\eta_{g,1}^{R}}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}\right).

(2.131)

Here, in the fourth and sixth equality,
we used the fact that
K_{h}^{R},\eta_{g,1}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}K_{h}^{R}\left(\eta_{g,1}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}}\right)

and
\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta}^{c}}\right)
commute, in order to remove
\Theta
.
Equation (

) and the fact that
\pi_{L}
is irreducible
imply that there is a unitary
v^{R}_{g,h}
satisfying (
2.129
).
The same argument implies the existence of
v^{L}_{g,h}
satisfying (
2.129
).

For this
v^{R}_{g,h}
, we would like to show (
2.130
).
Rewriting

\displaystyle\eta_{g,2}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\eta_{h,2}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh,2}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}=K_{g}^{\sigma}\cdot\left(\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}K_{h}^{\sigma}\left(\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\right)\cdot\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\eta_{h,1}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh,1}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(K_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{-1},

(2.132)

we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{L}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\eta_{g,2}^{R}\beta_{g}^{{R}U}\eta_{h,2}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{{R}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh,2}^{R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\circ K_{g}^{R}\cdot\left(\eta_{g,1}^{R}\beta_{g}^{{R}U}K_{h}^{R}\left(\eta_{g,1}^{R}\beta_{g}^{{R}U}\right)^{-1}\right)\cdot\eta_{g,1}^{R}\beta_{g}^{{R}U}\eta_{h,1}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{{R}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh,1}^{R}\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(K_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{L}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left({V_{g}^{R}v_{g,h}^{R}u_{R}^{(1)}(g,h)\left(V_{gh}^{R}\right)^{*}}\right)\pi_{R},

(2.133)

substituting
(
2.125
), (
2.131
), (

).
This proves (
2.130
) for
\sigma=R
.
Analogous result for
\sigma=L
can be proven by the same argument.
Hence we have proven the claim (
2.131
) and (
2.130
).

Setting

\displaystyle V_{g}:=V_{g}^{L}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{0}),

(2.134)

we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{g}W_{g,1}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ K_{g}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g,1}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g,2}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(2.135)

In the last equality, we used the definition of
K_{g}
and the commutativity of
\Theta
and
K_{g}
again.
From (
2.3
) and (
2.130
),
setting

\displaystyle u_{\sigma}^{(2)}(g,h):=V_{g}^{\sigma}v^{\sigma}_{g,h}u_{\sigma}^{(1)}(g,h)\left(V_{gh}^{\sigma}\right)^{*},

(2.136)

we see that

\displaystyle\left(\left(V_{g}W_{g,1}\right),\left({u_{R}^{(2)}}(g,h)\right)\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(2)}),(\eta_{g,2}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right),

(2.137)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(2)}}(g,h)=\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(1)}}(g,h)\left(V_{gh}^{R}\right)^{*}\right).

(2.138)

Now we set

\displaystyle c_{R,1}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(1)}),(\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g,1}),(u_{\sigma}^{(1)}(g,h))\right)\right),

\displaystyle c_{R,2}:=c_{R}\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta,(\tilde{\beta}_{g}^{(2)}),(\eta_{g,2}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left(\left(V_{g}W_{g,1}\right),\left({u_{R}^{(2)}}(g,h)\right)\right)\right).

(2.139)

To prove the Lemma, it suffices to show

.
By (

), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,h)u_{R}^{(2)}(gh,k)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(1)}}(g,h)\left(V_{gh}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{gh}^{R}\right)W_{gh,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{k}^{R}\right)\left(W_{gh,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(1)}}(gh,k)\left(V_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(1)}}(g,h)\right)\cdot W_{gh,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{k}^{R}\right)\left(W_{gh,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(1)}}(gh,k)\left(V_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes{u_{R}^{(1)}}(g,h)\right)\cdot W_{gh,1}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{k}^{R}\right)\right\}\cdot

(2.140)

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left[{u_{R}^{(1)}}(g,h){u_{R}^{(1)}}(gh,k)\right]\left(V_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R,1}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g,1}W_{h,1}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{k}^{R}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(1)}(h,k)\right)W_{g,1}^{*}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(1)}(g,hk)\left(V_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right).

2.58
) for
[\cdot]
part and

) for
\{\cdot\}
part
for the fourth equality.
Again using (

),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,h)u_{R}^{(2)}(gh,k)=(\ref {roe})

(2.142)

\displaystyle=c_{R,1}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)\left\{\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{h,1}\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{k}^{R}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(W_{h,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(V_{k}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\left(W_{h,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{h}^{R}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(h,k)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(V_{hk}^{R}\right)\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\right)

\displaystyle W_{g,1}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(V_{hk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,hk)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(V_{ghk}^{R}\right)\right)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\left(V_{ghk}^{R}\right)^{*}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R,1}(g,h,k)\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\cdot\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(W_{g,1}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)^{*}\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,hk)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R,1}(g,h,k)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes V_{g}^{R}\right)W_{g,1}\right)\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,hk)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R,1}(g,h,k)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left({V_{g}^{L}}^{*}\otimes\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{R}}\right)V_{g}W_{g,1}\right)\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,hk)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{R,1}(g,h,k)\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{g}W_{g,1}\right)\left(\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(h,k)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes u_{R}^{(2)}(g,hk)\right)

(2.143)

In the last line we used (
2.137
) and Lemma
2.3
(iii)
to remove
{V_{g}^{L}}^{*}
.
From this, we see that
c_{R,1}=c_{R,2}
, completing the proof.

\square

Lemma 2.17
.

Let

\displaystyle\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}<\frac{\pi}{2},\;\text{with}\;\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta_{1}),\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta_{2})\neq\emptyset.

(2.144)

Then
we have

\displaystyle h^{(5)}\left(\omega,\theta_{1}\right)=h^{(5)}\left(\omega,\theta_{2}\right).

(2.145)

Definition 2.18
.

From this lemma, for any
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
,
we may
define

\displaystyle h\left(\omega\right):=h^{(5)}\left(\omega,\theta\right)

(2.146)

independent of the choice of
\theta
.
This is the index we associate to
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
.

By the assumption, there are
some
(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta_{1})

and
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left((\theta_{1},\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right)
.
Because
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
, there are

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)

and
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{2}}

by the definition.
Setting
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}
, we have
\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta
.
Because
0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}<\frac{\pi}{2}
,
we also have
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left((\theta_{2},\tilde{\beta}_{g})\right)
, and

(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta_{2})
.
For the same reason, we also have

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{1}}
.

By Lemma
2.1
, there is some

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta_{1},(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(2.147)

However,
we also have

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta_{2},(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(2.148)

Therefore, we obtain

h^{(5)}\left(\omega,\theta_{1}\right)=h^{(5)}\left(\omega,\theta_{2}\right)
.

\square

This completes the proof of Theorem
2.7
.

3
The existence of
\tilde{\beta}
for SPT phases

In this section, we
give a sufficient condition for
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)
to be non-empty.
We consider the same setting as in subsection
2.1
.

Theorem 3.1
.

For any
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
and
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
satisfying

\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha

for all
g\in G
,

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha,\theta)
is not empty.

In order to prove this theorem, we first show several general lemmas.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{A}},{\mathfrak{B}}
be UHF-algebras.
Let
\omega
be a pure state on
{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}
and

\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
states on
{\mathfrak{A}}
,

{\mathfrak{B}}
respectively.
Assume that
\omega
is quasi-equivalent to
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
.
Then for any pure states
\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\psi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}

on
{\mathfrak{A}}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}
, there are automorphisms

\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{A}}\right)
,

\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{B}}\right)
and
a unitary
u\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega=\left(\left(\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\right)\otimes\left(\psi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u).

(3.1)

If
\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
and
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
are quasi-equivalent,
then we may set
\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{\omega},\pi_{\omega},\Omega_{\omega})

({\mathcal{H}}_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}},\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}},\Omega_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}})

({\mathcal{H}}_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}},\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}},\Omega_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}})

be
GNS triples of
\omega
,
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
respectively.
Then
({\mathcal{H}}_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}},\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\otimes\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}},\Omega_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\otimes\Omega_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}})

is a GNS triple of
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
.
As
\omega
is quasi-equivalent to
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
,
there is a
*
-isomorphism

\tau:\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}({\mathfrak{A}})^{\prime\prime}\otimes\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}({\mathfrak{B}})^{\prime\prime}

such that
\tau\circ\pi_{\omega}=\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\otimes\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}
.
Because
\omega
is pure, we have
\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\omega})

and from the isomorphism
\tau
,
\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}({\mathfrak{A}})^{\prime\prime}\otimes\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}({\mathfrak{B}})^{\prime\prime}

is also a type
I
factor.
Then from Theorem 2.30 V
[
T
]
,
both of
\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}({\mathfrak{A}})^{\prime\prime}
and
\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}({\mathfrak{B}})^{\prime\prime}

are type
I
factors.
The restriction of
\tau
to
\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

implies a
*
-isomorphism from
\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

onto the type I factor
\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}({\mathfrak{A}})^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence we see that
\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is a type
I
-factor.
Therefore, from Theorem 1.31 V of
[
T
]
,
there are Hilbert spaces

{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}},{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
and a unitary
W:{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega}\to{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W\right)\left(\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)={\mathcal{B}}\left({\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\right)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}
.
Because
\omega
is pure, we also have

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W\right)\left(\pi_{\omega}\left({\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}})
.
From this, we see that there are irreducible representations

\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
of
{\mathfrak{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{B}}
on

{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W)\circ\pi_{\omega}=\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
.
Fix some unit vectors
\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
.
Then because of the irreducibility of
\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
and
\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
,

\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}:=\left\langle\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}},\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\left(\cdot\right)\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\right\rangle
and

\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}}:=\left\langle\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}},\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(\cdot\right)\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right\rangle

are pure states on
{\mathfrak{A}}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}
.
By Theorem 1.1 of
[
KOS
]
(originally proved by Powers
[
P
]
for UHF-algebras)
for any pure states
\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\psi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}

on
{\mathfrak{A}}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}
, there exist automorphisms

\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{A}})

\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{B}})

such that

\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}=\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}

\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}}=\psi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
.
Now for unit vectors
W^{*}\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right),\Omega_{\omega}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\omega}
,
by Kadison’s transitivity theorem and the irreducibility of
\pi_{\omega}
, there exists a unitary
u\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}\right)

such that
\pi_{\omega}(u)\Omega_{\omega}=W^{*}\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)
.
Substituting this, we obtain

\displaystyle\omega=\left\langle\Omega_{\omega},\pi_{\omega}\left(\cdot\right)\Omega_{\omega}\right\rangle=\left\langle\pi_{\omega}(u^{*})W^{*}\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right),\pi_{\omega}\left(\cdot\right)\pi_{\omega}(u^{*})W^{*}\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\right\rangle=\left\langle W^{*}\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right),\pi_{\omega}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u)\left(\cdot\right)W^{*}\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right),\left(\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u)\left(\cdot\right)\left(\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\xi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\right\rangle=\left(\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u)=\left(\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u).

(3.2)

Now assume that
\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
and
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
are quasi-equivalent, i.e.,
the GNS representations of
\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
,
\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
, denoted by

\pi_{\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}
and
\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}
are quasi-equivalent.
From the above argument,
\pi_{\omega}|_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
and
\pi_{\varphi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}

are quasi-equivalent. At the same time,
\pi_{\omega}|_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
and

\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
are quasi-equivalent.
Therefore,
\pi_{\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}
and
\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
are quasi-equivalent.
Because both of them are irreducible, we
see that a pure state
{\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}
can be represented by a unit vector
\zeta\in{\mathcal{K}}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}

as
{\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}=\left\langle\zeta,\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\left(\cdot\right)\zeta\right\rangle
.
Because
\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
is irreducible,
by Kadison’s transitivity theorem, there exists a unitary
w\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\right)
such that

\rho_{{\mathfrak{A}}}(w^{*})\zeta=\xi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
.
Hence we obtain
{\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(w)=\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
.
Substituting this instead of
\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}=\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}

in (
3
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\omega=\left(\psi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(w\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)u\right),

(3.3)

proving the last claim.

\square

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{B}},{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L},{\mathfrak{A}}_{2,L},{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R},{\mathfrak{A}}_{2,R}
be UHF-algebras.
Set
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}:={\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R}
,

{\mathfrak{A}}_{2}:={\mathfrak{A}}_{2,L}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{2,R}
,

{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}:={\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{2,L}
, and

{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}:={\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{2,R}
. Let

\omega
,
\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}
,
\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}
,
\psi

be pure states on
{\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}
,

{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}
, respectively.
Suppose that

\omega
is quasi-equivalent to
\left.\left(\psi\otimes\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}}
.
Then for any pure states
\varphi_{L}^{(1)}
,
\varphi_{R}^{(1)}

on
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R}
respectively,
there are automorphisms
\gamma_{L}^{(1)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L}\right)
,

\gamma_{R}^{(1)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R}\right)
,
and a unitary
u\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega=\left(\psi\otimes\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1)}\circ\gamma_{L}^{(1)}\right)\otimes\left(\varphi_{R}^{(1)}\circ\gamma_{R}^{(1)}\right)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits u.

(3.4)

Because the pure state
\omega
is quasi-equivalent to
\left.\left(\psi\otimes\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}}=\psi\otimes\left.\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}}
, applying Lemma
3.2
,
for any pure states
\varphi_{L}^{(1)}
,
\varphi_{R}^{(1)}

on
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R}
,
there exist an automorphism
S\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}
and a unitary
v\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega=\left(\psi\otimes\left(\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1)}\right)\circ S\right)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits v.

(3.5)

From (
3.5
) and
\omega\sim_{q.e.}\left.\left(\psi\otimes\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}}
,
we get

\left(\psi\otimes\left(\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1)}\right)\circ S\right)\right)\sim_{q.e.}\left.\left(\psi\otimes\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}}
,
which implies

\displaystyle\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1)}\right)\circ S\sim_{q.e.}\left.\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}}.

(3.6)

Applying Lemma
3.2
to (
3.6
),
there are automorphisms
\gamma_{L}^{(1)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{1,L}\right)
,

\gamma_{R}^{(1)}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{1,R}\right)
,
and a unitary
w\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}_{1}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1)}\otimes\varphi_{R}^{(1)}\right)\circ S=\left(\left(\varphi_{L}^{(1)}\circ\gamma_{L}^{(1)}\right)\otimes\left(\varphi_{R}^{(1)}\circ\gamma_{R}^{(1)}\right)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits w.

(3.7)

Substituting this to (
3.5
), we obtain
(
3.4
).

\square

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}
,

{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}
,
{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}
be UHF-algebras, and set

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}:={\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU},\quad{\mathfrak{B}}_{D}:={\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD},\quad{\mathfrak{B}}_{L}:={\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU},\quad{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}:={\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU},\

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{A}}:={\mathfrak{A}}_{L}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{R},\quad{\mathfrak{B}}:={\mathfrak{B}}_{D}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}={\mathfrak{B}}_{L}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{R},\quad{\mathfrak{C}}:={\mathfrak{C}}_{D}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{U},\quad{\mathfrak{D}}:={\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}.

(3.8)

Let
\omega_{X}
be a pure state on
each
X={\mathfrak{A}}_{L},{\mathfrak{A}}_{R},{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU},{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD},{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU},{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD},{\mathfrak{C}}_{U},{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}
, and
set

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}:=\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}},\quad\text{on}\quad{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}:=\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}},\quad\text{on}\quad{\mathfrak{B}}_{D}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{D},

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}:=\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\quad\text{on}\quad{\mathfrak{A}}

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}:=\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}}\quad\text{on}\quad{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{L}

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}:=\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}}\quad\text{on}\quad{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{R}

\displaystyle\omega_{0}:=\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}X={\mathfrak{A}}_{L},{\mathfrak{A}}_{R},{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU},{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD},\\
{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU},{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD},{\mathfrak{C}}_{U},{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}\end{subarray}}\omega_{X},\quad\text{on}\quad{\mathfrak{D}}.

(3.9)

Let
\alpha,\hat{\alpha}

be automorphisms on
{\mathfrak{D}}
which allow the following decompositions

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}=\left(\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\right)\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}})

(3.10)

\displaystyle\alpha=\left(\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{D}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right)\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\otimes\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right)\circ({\rm{inner}}).

(3.11)

Here,
\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}
/
\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}

are automorphisms on
{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}
/
{\mathfrak{B}}_{D}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}
respectively.
For each
\sigma=L,R
,

\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{\sigma},\hat{\gamma}_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{\sigma}

are automorphisms on
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\sigma}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\sigma}D}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\sigma}U}
.
Suppose that
\omega_{0}\circ\hat{\alpha}=\omega_{0}
.
Then there are automorphisms
\eta_{L},\eta_{R}
on
{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}
,

{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}

such that

\omega_{0}\circ\alpha
is quasi-equivalent to

\omega_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\eta_{L}\otimes\eta_{R}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right)
.

First we claim that there are automorphisms
\theta_{\mathfrak{B}}^{LU}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}}
,

\theta_{\mathfrak{B}}^{RU}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}

and a unitary
u\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{B}}^{U}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}^{U}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}=\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\left(\theta_{{\mathfrak{B}}}^{LU}\otimes\theta_{{\mathfrak{B}}}^{RU}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}^{U}}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u\right).

(3.12)

To prove this, we first note that from
\omega_{0}\circ\hat{\alpha}=\omega_{0}

and the decomposition (
3.10
), we have

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\sim_{q.e.}\omega_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\circ\left(\widehat{\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}}\right)^{-1}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\circ\left(\widehat{\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}}\right)^{-1}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}.

(3.13)

From this, because both of the states above are pure, (hence the restrictions of
their GNS representations onto
{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}
are factors)
we have

\displaystyle\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}=\left.\left(\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}}\sim_{q.e.}\omega_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\left.\left(\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\circ\left(\widehat{\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}}\right)^{-1}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\circ\left(\widehat{\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}}\right)^{-1}\right)\right|_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{U}}.

(3.14)

We apply Lemma
3.3
for

{\mathfrak{B}}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1L}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{1R}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{2L}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{2R}
,

\omega
,
\varphi_{L}^{(1,2)}
,
\varphi_{R}^{(1,2)}
,
\psi

replaced by
{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}
,
{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}
,

{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}
,

\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}
,
\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\circ\left(\widehat{\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}}\right)^{-1}
,
\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\circ\left(\widehat{\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}}\right)^{-1}
,

\omega_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}

respectively.
From (
3.14
), they satisfy the conditions in Lemma
3.3
.
Applying Lemma
3.3
,
(for pure states
\varphi_{L}^{(1)}=\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}}
and

\varphi_{R}^{(1)}=\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}}
)
we obtain automorphisms
\theta_{{\mathfrak{B}}}^{LU}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}\right)
,

\theta_{{\mathfrak{B}}}^{RU}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}\right)
,
and a unitary
u\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{B}}_{U}\otimes{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}\right)

satisfying (
3.12
).

We set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\eta_{L}:=\left(\theta^{LU}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}}\right)\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{B}}_{LU}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}\right)\\
&\eta_{R}:=\left(\theta^{RU}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}}\right)\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathfrak{B}}_{RU}\otimes{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}\otimes{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}\right).\end{split}

(3.15)

Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\omega_{0}\circ\alpha=\left(\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\right)\circ\alpha\sim_{q.e.}\left(\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\circ\rho_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\right)\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\otimes\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right)\\
&\sim_{q.e.}\left(\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{U}\otimes\omega_{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{C}}}^{D}\right)\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\left(\left(\theta_{{\mathfrak{B}}}^{LU}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{L}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{LD}}\right)\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{L}\right)\otimes\left(\left(\theta_{{\mathfrak{B}}}^{RU}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{B}}_{RD}}\right)\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}{\mathfrak{B}}}^{R}\right)\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right)\\
&=\omega_{0}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{U}}\otimes\eta_{L}\otimes\eta_{R}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{D}}\right).\end{split}

(3.16)

This completes the proof.

\square

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
3.1
.

Let
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
and
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
satisfying

\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha

for all
g\in G
.
We would like to show that

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha,\theta)
is not empty.

Let us set
\theta_{2.2}:=\theta
and
consider

\theta_{0.8}
,
\theta_{1}
,
\theta_{1.2}
,
\theta_{1.8}
,
\theta_{2}
,

\theta_{2.8}
,
\theta_{3}
,
\theta_{3.2}

satisfying (
2.9
) for this
\theta_{2.2}
.
Because
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
, there is a decompotision
given by (
2.10
), (
2.11
), (
2.12
).
Using this decomposition, set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha_{1}:=\alpha_{1D}\otimes\alpha_{1U}\\
&\alpha_{1\zeta}:=\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],\zeta}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}],\zeta}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta_{0.8}}\right)^{c}\right)_{\zeta}}\right),\quad\zeta=U,D,\\
&\alpha_{2}:=\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(3.17)

We have

\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}
.

We would like to show that

\left(\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1},\alpha\circ\beta_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}\right)

satisfy the conditions of
(\alpha,\hat{\alpha})
in Lemma
3.4
.
We first show that they satisfy a decomposition corresponding to
(
3.10
) and (
3.11
).
For
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}^{2},H_{U}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\circ\alpha^{-1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\right)\left(\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}^{-1}.

(3.18)

The latter part
\left(\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}^{-1}
decomposes to left and right.
To see this, first note that

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}^{-1}\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1.2}}}\right).

(3.19)

Because the conjugation
\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\cdot\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}
does not change the
support of an automorphism,

\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}\right)\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}

is also supported on
{C_{\theta_{1.2}}}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\left(\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}\right)\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}^{-1}\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}^{-1}

(3.20)

Hence we get the left-right decomposition:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}^{-1}=\alpha_{1}\left(\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}\right)\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)\alpha_{1}^{-1}\alpha_{2}^{-1}\\
&=\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}^{-1}\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}^{-1}\\
&=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma_{\sigma}}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}^{-1}\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma_{\sigma}}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}^{-1}\right)\\
&=:\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\Xi_{\Gamma,g,\sigma}.\end{split}

(3.21)

Here we set

\displaystyle\Xi_{\Gamma,g,\sigma}=\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{\Gamma_{\sigma}}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}^{-1}\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\beta_{g}^{\Gamma_{\sigma}}\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}^{-1}\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}^{-1}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta_{2}}\right)_{\sigma}}\right).

(3.22)

On the other hand, the first part of
(
3.18
) with
\Gamma={\mathbb{Z}}^{2},H_{U}
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{g}^{-1}\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\xi_{D}\otimes\xi_{U},\quad\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{D}}}\otimes\xi_{U}\end{split}

(3.23)

where

\displaystyle\xi_{\zeta}:=\left(\beta_{g}^{\zeta}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{1,\zeta}\beta_{g}^{\zeta}\alpha_{1,\zeta}^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta_{0.8}}\right)^{c}\right)_{\zeta}}\right),\quad\zeta=U,D

(3.24)

Hence we obtain decompositions

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{D}}}\otimes\xi_{U}\right)\circ\left(\Xi_{H_{U},g,L}\otimes\Xi_{H_{U},g,R}\right),\\
&\left(\beta_{g}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\xi_{D}\otimes\xi_{U}\right)\circ\left(\Xi_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},g,L}\otimes\Xi_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},g,R}\right).\end{split}

(3.25)

Because
\xi_{\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(C_{\theta_{0.8}}\right)^{c}\right)_{\zeta}}\right)
commutes with
\beta_{g}^{C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}]}}

and
\beta_{g}^{C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}],U}}
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{H_{D}}}\otimes\beta_{g}^{C_{(\theta_{0.8},\frac{\pi}{2}],U}}\xi_{U}\right)\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}],L,U}}\Xi_{H_{U},g,L}\otimes\beta_{g}^{C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}],R,U}}\Xi_{H_{U},g,R}\right),\\
&\alpha\circ\beta_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{C_{(\theta_{0.8},\frac{\pi}{2}],D}}\xi_{D}\otimes\beta_{g}^{C_{(\theta_{0.8},\frac{\pi}{2}],U}}\xi_{U}\right)\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}],L}}\Xi_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},g,L}\otimes\beta_{g}^{C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}],R}}\Xi_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},g,R}\right).\end{split}

(3.26)

Furthermore, from
\beta_{g}
-invariance of

\omega_{0}\circ\alpha
, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}=\omega_{0}.

(3.27)

Now we apply Lemma
3.4

for
{\mathfrak{A}}_{\sigma}
,
{\mathfrak{B}}_{\sigma\zeta}
,

{\mathfrak{C}}_{\zeta}
replaced by
{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{[0,\theta_{0.8}]}\right)_{\sigma}}

{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{2}]}\right)_{\sigma,\zeta}}

{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{(\theta_{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)_{\zeta}}
,
for
\sigma=L,R
,
\zeta=D,U
.
By (
3.27
) and (
3.26
),

\left(\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1},\alpha\circ\beta_{g}\circ\alpha^{-1}\right)

satisfy the conditions of
(\alpha,\hat{\alpha})
in Lemma
3.4
,
for
\omega_{0}
and its restrictions.
Applying Lemma
3.4
, there are
\tilde{\eta}_{\sigma,g}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta_{2}}\right)_{\sigma}}\right)
,
g\in G
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1}\sim_{q.e.}\omega_{0}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{Lg}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg}\right),\quad g\in G.

(3.28)

Because both of
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1}
and

\omega_{0}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{Lg}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg}\right)

are pure,
by Kadison’s transitibity theorem, there exists
a unitary
\tilde{v}_{g}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{A}})

such
that

\displaystyle\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1}=\omega_{0}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\tilde{v}_{g}}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{Lg}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg}\right),\quad g\in G.

(3.29)

We define

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{g}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\alpha^{-1}\left(\tilde{v}_{g^{-1}}\right)\right)\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{Lg^{-1}}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg^{-1}}\right)\circ\alpha\circ\beta_{g}^{U},\quad g\in G.

(3.30)

It suffices to show that
(\tilde{\beta}_{g})\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha,\theta)=\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha,\theta_{2.2})
.
By (
3.29
), we have
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha
.
Therefore, what is left to be proven is that
there are

\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left(\left(C_{\theta}\right)_{\sigma}\right)
,
g\in G
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{g}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U},\;\text{for all}\;g\in G

(3.31)

By the decomposition (
2.10
) and the fact that

\tilde{\eta}_{Lg^{-1}}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg^{-1}}

has support in
C_{\theta_{2}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha^{-1}\circ\left(\tilde{\eta}_{Lg^{-1}}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg^{-1}}\right)\circ\alpha\\
&=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)^{-1}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{Lg^{-1}}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{Rg^{-1}}\right)\left(\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\right)\\
&=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\right),\end{split}

(3.32)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\eta_{g}^{\sigma}=\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],\sigma}\right)^{-1}\left(\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}\right)^{-1}\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\sigma g^{-1}}\right)\left(\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}\right)\circ\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],\sigma}\right)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left(\left(C_{\theta_{2.2}}\right)_{\sigma}\right),\quad\sigma=L,R\end{split}

(3.33)

Substituting this to (
3.30
), we obtain (
3.31
).
This completes the proof.

\square

4
The stability of the index
h(\omega)

In this section we prove the stability of the index
h(\omega)

with respect to
\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)
.
Then we have
\omega\circ\gamma\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega\circ\gamma)\neq\emptyset

and

\displaystyle h(\omega\circ\gamma)=h(\omega).

(4.1)

Step 1.

From
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
, there is an

\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
.
For any
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
fixed,
we show that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\theta}_{\alpha\circ\gamma}\neq\emptyset
, hence

\alpha\circ\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
and

\omega\circ\gamma=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\gamma\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
.
Set
\theta_{1.2}:=\theta
and choose

\displaystyle 0<\theta_{0}<\theta_{0.8}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{1.2}:=\theta<\theta_{1.8}<\theta_{2}<\theta_{2.2}<\theta_{2.8}<\theta_{3}<\theta_{3.2}<\frac{\pi}{2}.

(4.2)

Because
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
, there exists some

(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{2}}
.
Setting
\alpha_{0}:=\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}
, we have
\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta
.
Because
\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)
,
there are

\gamma_{H}\in\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
and

\gamma_{C}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})

such that

\displaystyle\gamma=\gamma_{C}\circ\gamma_{H}.

(4.3)

Because
\gamma_{H}\in\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
, we may decompose
\gamma_{H}

as

\displaystyle\gamma_{H}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{H,{L}}\otimes\gamma_{H,R}\right)=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}

(4.4)

with
some
\gamma_{H,{\sigma}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\left(C_{\theta_{0}}\right)_{\sigma}}}\right)
,
\sigma=L,R
.
We set
\gamma_{0}:=\gamma_{H,{L}}\otimes\gamma_{H,R}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{0}}}}\right)
.
By definition,
\gamma_{C}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
, allows a
decomposition

\displaystyle\gamma_{C}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{CS}

\displaystyle\gamma_{CS}=\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)

(4.5)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma_{X}:=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R,\zeta=D,U}\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\quad\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}:=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma},\quad\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}:=\bigotimes_{\zeta=D,U}\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}\\
&\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{X,\sigma,\zeta}}\right),\quad\gamma_{X,\sigma}:=\bigotimes_{\zeta=U,D}\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta},\quad\gamma_{X,\zeta}:=\bigotimes_{\sigma=L,R}\gamma_{X,\sigma,\zeta}\\
&\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}}\right),\quad\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}}\right),\end{split}

(4.6)

for

\displaystyle X=(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}],\quad\sigma=L,R,\quad\zeta=D,U.

(4.7)

Here we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{I}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}=\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\gamma_{I}\;\quad\text{for all}\quad g\in G,

(4.8)

for any

\displaystyle I=[0,\theta_{1}],(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],\left(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}\right],(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}].

(4.9)

Set

\displaystyle\hat{\Theta}:=\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1.8}}^{c}}\right)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1.2}}^{c}}\right),

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}_{\sigma}:=\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\circ\gamma_{H,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}),\quad\sigma=L,R.

(4.11)

We claim

\displaystyle\alpha\circ\gamma=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Theta}.

(4.12)

This means
(\hat{\alpha}_{L},\hat{\alpha}_{R},\hat{\Theta})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha\gamma}^{\theta_{1.2}}
,
hence
{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha\gamma}^{\theta}={\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha\gamma}^{\theta_{1.2}}\neq\emptyset
.
The claim (
4.12
) can be checked as follows.
Note that
\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}
and
\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}

commute because of their disjoint supports.
Because

\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{2}}^{c}}\right)
,
it commutes with
\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}

and
\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\alpha\circ\gamma=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\circ\hat{\Theta}\circ\gamma_{0}

(4.13)

Because
\gamma_{0}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{0}}}}\right)

and

\hat{\Theta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{1.8}}^{c}}\right)
commute,
we have

\displaystyle\alpha\circ\gamma=(\ref {paris})=({\rm{inner}})\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\circ\gamma_{0}\circ\hat{\Theta}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Theta},

(4.14)

proving (
4.12
).

Step 2.

From
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega)\neq\emptyset
, we fix a

0<\theta_{0}<\frac{\pi}{2}
such that

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta_{0})\neq\emptyset
.
We choose
\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1},\theta_{1.2},\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2},\theta_{2.2},\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3},\theta_{3.2}
such that

\displaystyle 0<\theta_{0}<\theta_{0.8}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{1.2}<\theta_{1.8}<\theta_{2}<\theta_{2.2}<\theta_{2.8}<\theta_{3}<\theta_{3.2}<\frac{\pi}{2}.

(4.15)

For these
\theta
s, we associate the decomposition of
\gamma
in
Step 1
.
Fix
\left(\tilde{\beta}_{g}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega,\theta_{0})
and

(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}(\theta_{0},(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
.
Set
\eta_{g}:=\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}
.
Note that
(\eta_{g}^{\sigma})
also belongs to

{\mathcal{T}}(\theta_{2},(\tilde{\beta}_{g}))
.
Set

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}:=\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\gamma_{H,\sigma}\right)^{-1}\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma}\gamma_{H,\sigma}\left(\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\right)^{-1}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta_{1.2}}\right)_{\sigma}}\right),

(4.16)

for
\sigma=L,R
.
We also set
\hat{\eta}_{g}:=\hat{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\hat{\eta}_{g}^{R}
.
We claim that
\left(\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega\circ\gamma,\theta_{1.2})

with
(\hat{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta_{1.2},\left(\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma\right)\right)
.
Clearly we have

\displaystyle\omega\circ\gamma\circ\left(\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma\right)=\omega\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}\circ\gamma=\omega\circ\gamma.

(4.17)

Therefore, what remains to be shown is

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\hat{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\hat{\eta}_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U}

(4.18)

To see this, we first have

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\gamma

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\circ\eta_{g}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\gamma_{0}

(4.19)

from the decomposition (
4.3
), (
4.4
) (
4
).
Because
\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}
commutes with

\eta_{g}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{0}}}}\right)
and

\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}

commutes with
\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}\eta_{g}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{1}}}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\gamma

\displaystyle=(\ref {sweden})=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)\gamma_{0}

(4.20)

On the other hand, because
\gamma_{CS}
and
\beta_{g}^{U}
commute, we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\gamma=({\rm{inner}})\gamma_{0}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{CS}^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\gamma_{CS}\gamma_{0}=({\rm{inner}})\gamma_{0}^{-1}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\gamma_{0}.

(4.21)

Combining (
4.20
) and (
4.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)\gamma_{0}\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\hat{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\hat{\eta}_{g}^{R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U}.

(4.22)

In the second equality, we used the fact that
\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}

and
\beta_{g}^{U}
commute.
This completes the proof of the claim.

Step 3.

We use the setting and notation of
Step 1.
(with
\theta_{0}
chosen in
Step 2.
)
and
Step. 2
.
By Lemma
2.1
, there exists

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega,\alpha,\theta_{2},(\tilde{\beta}_{g}),(\eta_{g}^{\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(4.23)

Now we have

\displaystyle\omega\circ\gamma\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits,\;\alpha\circ\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega\circ\gamma),\;\left(\gamma^{-1}\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g}\circ\gamma\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega\circ\gamma,\theta_{1.2}),\;(\hat{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta_{1.2},\left(\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma\right)\right),\;(\hat{\alpha}_{L},\hat{\alpha}_{R},\hat{\Theta})\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha\gamma}^{\theta_{1.2}}.

(4.24)

We claim

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(u_{\sigma}(g,h))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega\circ\gamma,\alpha\circ\gamma,\theta_{1.2},(\gamma^{-1}\tilde{\beta}_{g}\gamma),(\hat{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}),(\hat{\alpha}_{L},\hat{\alpha}_{R},\hat{\Theta})\right).

(4.25)

This immediately implies
h(\omega)=h(\omega\circ\gamma)
.
To prove the claim, we first see from (
4.10
) and (
4.11
) that

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Theta}\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\circ\gamma_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\right)\circ\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}

(4.26)

because
\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\circ\gamma_{0}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{1.2}}}}\right)

and
\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{1.8}}}^{c}}\right)

commute.
Furthermore, because
\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}

and
\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{1.8}}}^{c}}\right)

commute,
while
\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}
and

\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{2}}}^{c}}\right)

commute,
we have

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Theta}\circ\gamma_{0}^{-1}\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\right)^{-1}=(\ref {greece})=\alpha_{0}\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\circ\Theta\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)=\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\hat{\gamma}.

(4.27)

Here
\hat{\gamma}:=\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{1}}}^{c}}\right)

commutes with
\beta_{g}^{U}
.
Combining this and

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}=\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\gamma_{0}\right)^{-1}\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\gamma_{0},

(4.28)

we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\circ\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Theta}\circ\hat{\eta}_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\hat{\Theta}\right)^{-1}\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)^{-1}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\hat{\gamma}\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(4.29)

Because

\hat{\gamma}

commutes with
\beta_{g}^{U}

and
\eta_{g}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{0}}}}\right)
commutes with

\hat{\gamma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{1}}}^{c}}\right)
,
we have

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\circ\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)\circ\hat{\Theta}\circ\hat{\eta}_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\hat{\Theta}\right)^{-1}\left(\hat{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\hat{\alpha}_{R}\right)^{-1}=(\ref {madrid})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

(4.30)

Hence the condition for
W_{g}
in (
4.25
) is checked.
On the other hand,
substituting (
4.11
) and (
4.16
), we get

\displaystyle\pi_{R}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{R}\circ\hat{\eta}_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\hat{\eta}_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\hat{\eta}_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\hat{\alpha}_{R}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],R}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],R}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],R}\circ\gamma_{H,R}\circ\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],R}\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],R}\circ\gamma_{H,R}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],R}\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],R}\circ\gamma_{H,R}\circ\left(\left(\gamma_{[0,\theta_{1}],R}\otimes\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],R}\right)\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],R}\circ\gamma_{H,R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\circ\left(\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],R}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{R}^{-1}.

(4.31)

Because

\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{C_{\theta_{0}}}}\right)
commutes with
\gamma_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],R}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{R}\circ\hat{\alpha}_{R}\circ\hat{\eta}_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\hat{\eta}_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\hat{\eta}_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\hat{\alpha}_{R}^{-1}=(\ref {totori})=\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\eta_{h}^{R}\left(\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\left(\eta_{gh}^{R}\right)^{-1}\alpha_{R}^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{R}(g,h)\right)\circ\pi_{R}.

(4.32)

An analogous statement for
\sigma=L
also holds.
This completes the proof of (
4.25
).
Hence the statement of the Theorem is proven.

\square

5
Proof of Theorem
1.4

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.4
.
The proof relies heavily on the machinery of quasi-local automorphisms
developed in
[
BMNS
]

[
NSY
]
,
[
MO
]
.
(Summary is given in Appendix
D
.)
We use terminology and facts from Appendix
C
,
D
, freely.
We introduce a set of
F
-functions with fast decay,
{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
as
Definition
C.2
.
Crucial point for us is the following.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
and
\omega_{\Phi_{0}}
,
\omega_{\Phi_{1}}

be their unique gapped ground states.
Suppose that

\Phi_{0}\sim\Phi_{1}
holds, via a path
\Phi:[0,1]\to{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
.
Then there exists some

\Psi\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
with
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
for some
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
of the form

F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a constant
0<\theta<1
,
such that

\omega_{\Phi_{1}}=\omega_{\Phi_{0}}\circ\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}
.
If

\Phi_{0},\Phi_{1}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG\beta}
and

\Phi\sim_{\beta}\Phi_{0}
,
we may take
\Psi
to be
\beta
-invariant.

For the proof,
see Appendix
D
.

From this and Theorem
3.1
and Theorem
4.1
,
in order to show Theorem
1.4
, it suffices to show
the following.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let

F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
be an
F
-function of the form

F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a constant
0<\theta<1
.
Let
\Psi\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
be a path of interactions satisfying
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
.
Then we have
\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}\in\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Furthermore, if
\Psi
is
\beta_{g}^{U}
-invariant, i.e.,

\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\Psi(X;t)\right)=\Psi(X;t)
for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
, and

g\in G
, then
we have
\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.

Fix arbitrary

\displaystyle 0<\theta_{0.8}<\theta_{1}<\theta_{1.2}<\theta_{1.8}<\theta_{2}<\theta_{2.2}<\theta_{2.8}<\theta_{3}<\theta_{3.2}<\frac{\pi}{2}.

(5.1)

We show the existence of the decomposition

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}=&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u)\circ\left(\alpha_{(0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\right)\\
&\circ\left(\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\right),\end{split}

(5.2)

with
\alpha
s of the form (
2.11
) and (
2.12
).
We follow the strategy in
[
NO
]
.

Step 1.

Fix some
0<\theta^{\prime}<\theta
, and set

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(r):=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta^{\prime}}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}.

(5.3)

With suitably chosen constant
c_{1}>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{F\left(\frac{r}{3}\right),\left(F\left(\left[\frac{r}{3}\right]\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\leq c_{1}\tilde{F}(r),\quad\quad r\geq 0.

(5.4)

Namely,
c_{1}\tilde{F}(r)
satisfy the condition of
\tilde{F}_{\theta}

in Definition
C.2
(ii) for our
F=\frac{\exp\left({-cr^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
and
\theta=\frac{1}{2}
.

Set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{0}:=\left\{\begin{gathered}C_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}\;,C_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma,\zeta}\;,C_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],\sigma,\zeta}\;,C_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta},\\
\sigma=L,R,\quad\zeta=D,U.\end{gathered}\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{1}:=\left\{\begin{gathered}C_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}),\sigma,\zeta}\;,C_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}),\sigma,\zeta}\;,C_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}),\sigma,\zeta},\\
\sigma=L,R,\quad\zeta=D,U.\end{gathered}\right\}.

Define
\Psi^{(0)},\Psi^{(1)}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Psi^{(0)}\left(X;t\right):=\left\{\begin{gathered}\Psi\left(X;t\right),\quad\text{if there exists a}\quad C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\quad\text{such that }\quad X\subset C\\
0,\quad\text{otherwise}\end{gathered}\right.,\\
&\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right):=\Psi^{(0)}\left(X;t\right)-\Psi\left(X;t\right),\end{split}

(5.9)

for each
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
.

First we would like to represent
\left(\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}\circ\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}

as some quasi-local automorphism.
Let
t,s\in[0,1]
.
We apply Proposition
D.6
for
\Psi
replaced by
\Psi^{(1)}
,and
\tilde{\Psi}

by
\Psi
.
Hence we set

\displaystyle\Xi^{(s)}\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,\;X(m)=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi}\left(\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right)\right)

(5.10)

and

\displaystyle\Xi^{(n)(s)}\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,X(m)\cap\Lambda_{n}=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n})\Psi}\left(\Psi^{{(1)}}\left(X;t\right)\right)\right).

(5.11)

Corresponding to (
D.31
), we obtain

\displaystyle\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi}\left(H_{\Lambda_{n},\Psi^{(1)}}(t)\right)=H_{\Lambda_{n},\Xi^{(n)(s)}}(t).

(5.12)

Applying Proposition
D.6
.
we have
\Xi^{(n)(s)},\Xi^{(s)}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
, and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\tau_{t,u}^{\Xi^{(n)(s)}}\left(A\right)-\tau_{t,u}^{\Xi^{(s)}}\left(A\right)\right\|=0,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}},\quad t,u\in[0,1]

(5.13)

holds.
Two functions
\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(n)(s)}}(A)
and
\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi}\circ\left(\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}(A)

satisfy the same differential equation and the initial condition.
Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(n)(s)}}(A)=\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi}\circ\left(\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}(A),\quad t\in[0,1],\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

(5.14)

From the fact that
\hat{\tau}_{t,u}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(n)(s)}}=\tau_{u,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(n)(s)}}=\tau_{u,t}^{\Xi^{(n)(s)}}
converges strongly to an automorphism
\tau_{u,t}^{\Xi^{(s)}}
on

{\mathcal{A}}
(
5.13
),
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n})\Xi^{(n)(s)}}\left(A\right)-\tau_{s,t}^{\Xi^{(s)}}\left(A\right)\right\|=0,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

(5.15)

On the other hand, by Theorem
D.3
,
we have for
t\in[0,1]
and
A\in{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi}\circ\left(\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}(A)-\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi}\circ\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}(A)\right\|=0.

(5.16)

Therefore, taking
n\to\infty
limit in (
5.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\tau_{s,t}^{\Xi^{(s)}}(A)=\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi}\circ\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}(A),\quad t,s\in[0,1],\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

(5.17)

Hence we have

\displaystyle\tau_{s,t}^{\Psi}=\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi}\right)^{-1}=\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}\left(\tau_{s,t}^{\Xi^{(s)}}\right)^{-1}=\tau_{s,t}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\tau_{t,s}^{\Xi^{(s)}}

(5.18)

In particular, we get

\displaystyle\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}=\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\tau_{0,1}^{\Xi^{(1)}}.

(5.19)

Step 2.

We show

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2}),\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.Z\subset C\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Xi^{(1)}\left(Z,t\right)\right\|<\infty.

(5.20)

From this,

\displaystyle V(t):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2}),\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.Z\subset C\end{subarray}}\Xi^{(1)}\left(Z,t\right)\in{\mathcal{A}}

(5.21)

converges absolutely in the norm topology
and define an element in
{\mathcal{A}}
.
Furthermore, for

\displaystyle V_{n}(t):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2}),\;Z\subset\Lambda_{n}\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.Z\subset C\end{subarray}}\Xi^{(1)}\left(Z,t\right)\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}},\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

(5.22)

we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|V_{n}(t)-V(t)\right\|=0,

(5.23)

from (
5.20
).

To prove (
5.20
), we first bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2}),\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.Z\subset C\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Xi^{(1)}\left(Z,t\right)\right\|\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2}),\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.Z\subset C\end{subarray}}\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:X\subset Z,\\
X(m)=Z\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,1}^{\Psi}\left(\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right)\right)\right\|\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.X(m)\subset C\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,1}^{\Psi}\left(\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right)\right)\right\|\\
&\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.X(m)\subset C\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\frac{8\left\|\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right\|}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]\\
&=\frac{8}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1\right)\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
\not\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}s.t.X(m)\subset C\end{subarray}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right].\end{split}

(5.24)

For the third inequality, we used Theorem
D.3
3.
For any cone
C_{1},C_{2}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
with apex at the origin, we set

\displaystyle M(C_{1},C_{2}):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1},X\cap\left(\left(C^{c}\right)(m)\right)\neq\emptyset,\\
X\cap C_{1}\neq\emptyset,\hskip 8.19447ptX\cap C_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right].

(5.25)

From the definition of
\Psi^{(1)}
, we have

\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)=0,

unless

X
has a non-empty intersection with at least two of
elements in
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
.
Therefore, if
X
gives a non-zero contribution in
(
5.24
), then it has to satisfy

\displaystyle X\cap\left(\left(C^{c}\right)(m)\right)\neq\emptyset,\quad\text{for all}\quad C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1},

\displaystyle\exists C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\quad\text{such that},\;C_{1}\neq C_{2},\ \quad X\cap C_{1}\neq\emptyset,\quad X\cap C_{2}\neq\emptyset.

Hence we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\ref {remi})\leq\frac{8}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\\
C_{1}\neq C_{2}\end{subarray}}M({C_{1},C_{2}})\end{split}

(5.26)

Hence it suffice so show that

M({C_{1},C_{2}})<\infty
for all
C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}

with
C_{1}\neq C_{2}
.

In order to proceed, we prepare two estimates.
We will freely identify
{\mathbb{C}}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
in an obvious manner.
In particular,
\arg z
of
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

in the following definition
is considered with this identification:
for
\varphi_{1}<\varphi_{2}
, we set

\displaystyle\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}:=\left\{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\mid\arg z\in[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]\right\}.

(5.27)

We define
\check{C}_{(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})}
etc. analogously.
Set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{c^{(0)}}_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}}:=\sqrt{1-\max\left\{\cos(\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{2}),\cos(\zeta_{4}-\zeta_{1}),0\right\}},\quad\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}\in{\mathbb{R}}.\end{split}

(5.28)

Lemma 5.3
.
