Let
\varphi_{1}<\varphi_{2}<\varphi_{3}<\varphi_{4}
with
\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{1}<2\pi
.
Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b_{0}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4})\\
&:=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset,\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]\\
&\leq(64)^{3}\frac{3^{4}\kappa_{1,4,F}}{\left(c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}\right)^{4}}\left(\left\|\left|\Psi_{1}\right\|\right|_{F}\right)\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}G_{F}\left(m\right)\right)<\infty.\end{split}

Substituting Lemme
C.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle b_{0}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4})

(5.29)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset,\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]},\\
y\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\end{subarray}}\sum_{X\ni x,y}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|\left|\Psi_{1}\right\|\right|_{F}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]},\\
y\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\end{subarray}}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}G_{F}\left(m\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq(64)^{3}\frac{3^{4}\kappa_{1,4,F}}{\left(c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}\right)^{4}}\left(\left\|\left|\Psi_{1}\right\|\right|_{F}\right)\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}G_{F}\left(m\right)\right)<\infty.

(5.30)

We used Lemma
5.3
at the last inequality.
The last value is finite by (
C.14
) for our
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
.

\square

Set

\displaystyle{c^{(1)}}_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3}}:=\sqrt{1-\max\left\{\cos(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}),\cos(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{3})\right\}},\quad\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3}\in[0,2\pi).

(5.31)

Lemma 5.4
.

For
\varphi_{1}<\varphi_{2}<\varphi_{3}
with
\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b_{1}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
X\subset\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{3}]}\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\neq\emptyset\\
X\cap\left(\left(\left(\check{C}_{(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{3})}\right)^{c}\right)(m)\right)\neq\emptyset\\
\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]\\
&\leq 64\cdot 144\cdot 24\cdot\left(\pi\kappa_{1,2,F}+F(0)\right)\left(\left\|\left|\Psi_{1}\right\|\right|_{F}\right)\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{4}G_{F}\left(m\right)\right)\left(\left({c^{(1)}}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}}\right)^{-4}+\left({c^{(1)}}_{\varphi_{3},\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}}\right)^{-4}\right)<\infty.\end{split}

(5.32)

Set

\displaystyle L_{\varphi}:=\left\{z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid\arg z=\varphi\right\},\quad\varphi\in[0,2\pi).

(5.33)

Note that
if
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
satisfies

X\subset\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{3}]}
and

X\cap\left(\left(\left(\check{C}_{(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{3})}\right)^{c}\right)(m)\right)\neq\emptyset
,
then
we have

\displaystyle d(X,L_{\varphi_{1}})\leq m,\quad\text{or}\quad d(X,L_{\varphi_{3}})\leq m.

(5.34)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
X\subset\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{3}]}\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\neq\emptyset\\
X\cap\left(\left(\left(\check{C}_{(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{3})}\right)^{c}\right)(m)\right)\neq\emptyset\\
\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}G_{F}\left(m\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\neq\emptyset\\
d(X,L_{\varphi_{1}})\leq m\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:\\
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset\\
d(X,L_{\varphi_{3}})\leq m\end{subarray}}\right)\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{m\geq 0}G_{F}\left(m\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\\
y\in L_{\varphi_{1}}(m)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\\
y\in L_{\varphi_{3}}(m)\end{subarray}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:X\ni x,y\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|\right]\\
&\leq\left(\left\|\left|\Psi_{1}\right\|\right|_{F}\right)\sum_{m\geq 0}G_{F}\left(m\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\\
y\in L_{\varphi_{1}}(m)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\\
y\in L_{\varphi_{3}}(m)\end{subarray}}\right)F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\\
&\leq 64\cdot 144\cdot 24\cdot\left(\pi\kappa_{1,2,F}+F(0)\right)\left(\left\|\left|\Psi_{1}\right\|\right|_{F}\right)\left(\sum_{m\geq 0}(m+1)^{4}G_{F}\left(m\right)\right)\left(\left({c^{(1)}}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}}\right)^{-4}+\left({c^{(1)}}_{\varphi_{3},\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}}\right)^{-4}\right)\\
\\
\end{split}

(5.35)

At the last inequality, we used Lemma
C.5
with

\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}
.
Because of
\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}
and (
C.14
), the last value is finite.

\square

Now let us go back to the estimate of (
5.25
).
If
C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
are

C_{1}=\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}
,

C_{2}=\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}

with
\varphi_{1}<\varphi_{2}<\varphi_{3}<\varphi_{4}
,
\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{1}<2\pi
,
then from Lemma
5.3
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&M(C_{1},C_{2})\leq b_{0}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4})<\infty.\end{split}

(5.36)

Now suppose that
C_{1},C_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
are

C_{1}=\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}
,

C_{2}=\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}

with
\varphi_{1}<\varphi_{2}<\varphi_{3}
,
\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{1}<2\pi
.
By the definition of
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
,
there is some
C=C_{(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}

such that
\varphi_{1}<\zeta_{1}<\varphi_{2}<\zeta_{2}<\varphi_{3}
and
\zeta_{2}-\zeta_{1}<\frac{\pi}{2}
.
For
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

to give a nonzero contribution in (
5.25
),
it have to satisfy

\displaystyle X(m)\cap\left(\check{C}_{[\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}]}\right)^{c}\neq\emptyset,\quad X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset,\quad X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\neq\emptyset.

(5.37)

For such an
X
, one of the following occurs:

(i)

X\cap\check{C}_{[\zeta_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\neq\emptyset
and
X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset

(ii)

X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\zeta_{1}]}\neq\emptyset
and

X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]}\neq\emptyset

(iii)

X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\zeta_{2}]}\neq\emptyset

(and

X\cap\check{C}_{[\zeta_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset
)
and

X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{1}+2\pi]}\neq\emptyset
,

(iv)

X\subset\check{C}_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}
,

X\cap\left(\left(\check{C}_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right)^{c}\right)(m)\neq\emptyset
,

X\cap\check{C}_{[\varphi_{2},\zeta_{2}]}\neq\emptyset
,
and

X\cap\check{C}_{[\zeta_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\neq\emptyset
.

Hence we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&M({C_{1},C_{2}})\\
&\leq b_{0}(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\zeta_{2},\varphi_{3})+b_{0}(\varphi_{1},\zeta_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3})+b_{0}(\varphi_{2},\zeta_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{1}+2\pi)\\
&+b_{1}(\zeta_{1},\varphi_{2},\zeta_{2})\\
&<\infty.\end{split}

(5.38)

Hence we have proven the claim of
Step 2
.

Step 3.

Next we set

\displaystyle\tilde{\Xi}(Z,t):=\left\{\begin{gathered}\Xi^{(1)}(Z,t),\quad\text{if}\;\quad\;\exists C\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\;s.t.\;Z\subset C\\
0\quad\text{otherwise}\end{gathered}.\right.

Clearly, we have
\tilde{\Xi}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
.
Note that

\displaystyle H_{\Lambda_{n},\tilde{\Xi}}(t)+V_{n}(t)=H_{\Lambda_{n},\Xi^{(1)}}(t).

(5.41)

As a uniform limit of
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto V_{n}(t)\in{\mathcal{A}}
, (
5.23
),

[0,1]\ni t\mapsto V(t)\in{\mathcal{A}}
is norm-continuous.
Because of
\tilde{\Xi}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
,

[0,1]\ni t\mapsto\tau_{t,s}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V(t)\right)\in{\mathcal{A}}
is also norm-continuous,
for each
s\in[0,1]
.
Therefore, for each
s\in[0,1]
, there is a unique norm-differentiable map

[0,1]\ni t\mapsto W^{(s)}(t)\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}W^{(s)}(t)=-i\tau_{t,s}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V(t)\right)W^{(s)}(t),\quad W^{(s)}(s)=\mathbb{I}.

(5.42)

It is given by

\displaystyle W^{(s)}(t):=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-i)^{k}\int_{s}^{t}ds_{1}\int_{s}^{s_{1}}ds_{2}\cdots\int_{s}^{s_{k-1}}ds_{k}\tau_{s_{1},s}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V(s_{1})\right)\cdots\tau_{s_{k},s}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V(s_{k})\right).

(5.43)

Analogously, for each
s\in[0,1]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
a unique norm-differentiable map from

[0,1]
to
{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}W_{n}^{(s)}(t)=-i\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n})\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V_{n}(t)\right)W_{n}^{(s)}(t),\quad W_{n}^{(s)}(s)=\mathbb{I}.

(5.44)

It is given by

\displaystyle W_{n}^{(s)}(t):=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-i)^{k}\int_{s}^{t}ds_{1}\int_{s}^{s_{1}}ds_{2}\cdots\int_{s}^{s_{k-1}}ds_{k}\tau_{s_{1},s}^{(\Lambda_{n})\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V_{n}(s_{1})\right)\cdots\tau_{s_{k},s}^{(\Lambda_{n})\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V_{n}(s_{k})\right).

(5.45)

By the uniform convergence (
5.23
) and Lemma
D.3
,
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n})\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V_{n}(t)\right)-\tau_{t,s}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V(t)\right)\right\|=0.

(5.46)

From this and (
5.43
), (
5.45
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|W_{n}^{(s)}(t)-W^{(s)}(t)\right\|=0.

(5.47)

This and Theorem
D.3
4 for
\Xi^{(1)},\tilde{\Xi}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])

imply

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{n\to\infty}\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)=\tau_{s,t}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W^{(s)}(t)\right)(A),\\
\lim_{n\to\infty}\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(1)}}(A)=\tau_{s,t}^{\Xi^{(1)}}(A),\end{split}

(5.48)

for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
.

Note that for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{d}{dt}\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)\\
&=-i\left[H_{\Lambda_{n},\tilde{\Xi}}(t),\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)\right]\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-i\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\left(\left[\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\left(V_{n}(t)\right),\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)\right]\right)\\
&=-i\left[H_{\Lambda_{n},\tilde{\Xi}}(t)+V_{n}(t),\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)\right]\\
&=-i\left[H_{\Lambda_{n},\Xi^{(1)}}(t),\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)\right].\end{split}

We used (
D.10
)
for the second equality and (
5.41
) for the third equality.
On the other hand, for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(1)}}(A)=-i\left[H_{\Lambda_{n},\Xi^{(1)}}(t),\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(1)}}(A)\right].

(5.49)

Therefore,
\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)

and
\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(1)}}(A)
satisfy the same differential equation.
Also note that we have
\tau_{s,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(s)\right)(A)=\tau_{s,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(1)}}(A)=A
.
Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{n}^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)=\tau_{s,t}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\Xi^{(1)}}(A).

(5.50)

By (
5.48
), we obtain

\displaystyle\tau_{s,t}^{\tilde{\Xi}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W^{(s)}(t)\right)(A)=\tau_{s,t}^{\Xi^{(1)}}(A),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}},\;t,s\in[0,1].

(5.51)

Taking inverse, we get

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W^{(s)^{*}}(t)\right)\circ\tau_{t,s}^{\tilde{\Xi}}=\tau_{t,s}^{\Xi^{(1)}},\;t,s\in[0,1].

(5.52)

Step 4.

Combining (
5.19
) and (
5.52
) we have

\displaystyle\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}=\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\tau_{0,1}^{\Xi^{(1)}}=\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(W^{(1)}(0)\right)^{*}\right)\circ\tau_{0,1}^{\tilde{\Xi}}.

(5.53)

By the definition of
\Psi^{(0)}
and
\tilde{\Xi}
,
we obtain
decompositions

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}=\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}]}\\
&\tau_{0,1}^{\tilde{\Xi}}=\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}]}\otimes\alpha_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}]}\end{split}

(5.54)

with
\alpha
s of the form (
2.11
) and (
2.12
).
This completes the proof of the first part.

Step 5.

Suppose that
\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\Psi(X;t)\right)=\Psi(X;t)
for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
, and

g\in G
. Then clearly we have
\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\Psi^{(0)}(X;t)\right)=\Psi^{(0)}(X;t)
for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
, and

g\in G
.
By Theorem
D.3
, 5, this implies

\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}{\beta_{g}^{U}}={\beta_{g}^{U}}\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}
.
From the decomposition (
5.54
),
this means all of
\alpha_{[0,\theta_{1}],\sigma}
,

\alpha_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}],\sigma,\zeta}
,
\alpha_{(\theta_{2},\theta_{3}],\sigma,\zeta}
,

\alpha_{(\theta_{3},\frac{\pi}{2}],\zeta}
,
\sigma=L,R
,
\zeta=U,D

commute with
{\beta_{g}^{U}}
.
Because
\Pi_{X}
commutes with
\beta_{g}^{U}

\tau_{t,s}^{\Psi}
commutes with
\beta_{g}^{U}
(Theorem
D.3
, 5)
, and
\Psi^{(1)}
is
\beta_{g}^{U}
-invariant,

\Xi^{(s)}
is
\beta_{g}^{U}
-invariant
from the definition (
5.10
).
Therefore, from the definition (
5
),
\tilde{\Xi}
is also

\beta_{g}^{U}
-invariant.
Hence by Theorem
D.3
, 5

\tau_{0,1}^{\tilde{\Xi}}
commutes with
{\beta_{g}^{U}}
.
The decomposition
(
5.54
) then implies that

\alpha_{(\theta_{0.8},\theta_{1.2}],\sigma,\zeta}

\alpha_{(\theta_{1.8},\theta_{2.2}],\sigma,\zeta}

\alpha_{(\theta_{2.8},\theta_{3.2}],\sigma,\zeta}
,
\sigma=L,R
,
\zeta=U,D

commute with
{\beta_{g}^{U}}
.

\square

An analogous proof shows the following.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let

F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
be an
F
-function of the form

F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a constant
0<\theta<1
.
Let
\Psi\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
be a path of interactions satisfying
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
.
Define
\Psi^{(0)}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Psi^{(0)}\left(X;t\right):=\left\{\begin{gathered}\Psi\left(X;t\right),\quad\text{if }\quad X\subset H_{U}\quad\text{or}\quad X\subset H_{D}\\
0,\quad\text{otherwise}\end{gathered}\right.,\end{split}

(5.55)

for each
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
.
Then

\left(\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.

Define
\tilde{F}
as in (
5.3
) with some
0<\theta^{\prime}<\theta
.
The same argument as in Theorem
5.2

Step 1.
implies
that there exists
\Xi^{(1)}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}[0,1]
with
\tilde{F}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
,
such that

\displaystyle\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}=\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\tau_{0,1}^{\Xi^{(1)}}.

(5.56)

This
\Xi^{(1)}
is given by the formula (
5.10
)
for current
\Psi
and
\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right):=\Psi^{(0)}\left(X;t\right)-\Psi\left(X;t\right)
.
To prove the theorem, it suffices to show that

\tau_{0,1}^{\Xi^{(1)}}
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Indeed, for any
0<\theta_{0}<\frac{\pi}{4}
,
as in Theorem
5.2

Step 2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z:Z\nsubseteq C_{[0,\theta_{0}],L}\\
\text{and}\;\;Z\nsubseteq C_{[0,\theta_{0}],R}\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Xi^{(1)}\left(Z,t\right)\right\|\\
&\leq\frac{8}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1\right)\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:X(m)\nsubseteq C_{[0,\theta_{0}],L}\\
\text{and}\;\;X(m)\nsubseteq C_{[0,\theta_{0}],R}\end{subarray}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Psi^{(1)}\left(X;t\right)\right\|\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right)\right]<\infty.\end{split}

(5.57)

To see this, note that if
X
in the last line has a non-zero contribution to the sum,
then at least one of the following occurs.

(i)

X\cap C_{[\theta_{0},\frac{\pi}{2}],U}\neq\emptyset
,
and
X\cap H_{D}\neq\emptyset

(ii)

X\cap C_{[\theta_{0},\frac{\pi}{2}],D}\neq\emptyset
,
and
X\cap H_{U}\neq\emptyset

(iii)

X\subset C_{[0,\theta_{0}]}
and

(1)

X\cap C_{[0,\theta_{0}],L}\neq\emptyset
and

X\cap C_{[0,\theta_{0}],R}\neq\emptyset
, or

(2)

X\subset C_{[0,\theta_{0}],R}
,

X\cap\check{C}_{[0,\theta_{0}]}\neq\emptyset
,

X\cap\check{C}_{[-\theta_{0},0]}\neq\emptyset

and
X{(m)}\cap\left(C_{[0,\theta_{0}],R}\right)^{c}\neq\emptyset
,

(3)

X\subset C_{[0,\theta_{0}],L}
,

X\cap\check{C}_{[\pi-\theta_{0},\pi]}\neq\emptyset
,

X\cap\check{C}_{[\pi,\pi+\theta_{0}]}\neq\emptyset

and
X{(m)}\cap\left(C_{[0,\theta_{0}],L}\right)^{c}\neq\emptyset
.

Therefore, the summation in the second line of (
5.57
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{8}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1\right)\left(\begin{gathered}b_{0}(\theta_{0},\pi-\theta_{0},\pi,2\pi)+b_{0}(0,\pi,\pi+\theta_{0},2\pi-\theta_{0})+b_{0}(-\theta_{0},\theta_{0},\pi-\theta_{0},\pi+\theta_{0})\\
+b_{1}(-\theta_{0},0,\theta_{0})+b_{1}(\pi-\theta_{0},\pi,\pi+\theta_{0})\end{gathered}\right)<\infty,

from Lemma
5.3
and
5.4
,
proving (
5.57
).

Therefore, as in
Step 3.
of Theorem
5.2
,
setting

\displaystyle\tilde{\Xi}(Z,t):=\left\{\begin{gathered}\Xi^{(1)}(Z,t),\quad\text{if}\quad Z\subseteq C_{[0,\theta_{0}],L}\quad\text{or}\;\;Z\subseteq C_{[0,\theta_{0}],R}\\
0\quad\text{otherwise}\end{gathered}\right.,

we obtain
\tau_{0,1}^{\Xi^{(1)}}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\tau_{0,1}^{\tilde{\Xi}}
.
By the definition,
\tau_{0,1}^{\tilde{\Xi}}
decomposes as

\tau_{0,1}^{\tilde{\Xi}}=\zeta_{L}\otimes\zeta_{R}
, with some
\zeta_{\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{[0,\theta_{0}],\sigma}}\right)
,
\sigma=L,R
.
As this holds for any
0<\theta_{0}<\frac{\pi}{4}
, we conclude
\tau_{0,1}^{\Xi^{(1)}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.

\square

Theorem 5.6
.

Let

F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
be an
F
-function of the form

F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a constant
0<\theta<1
.
Let
\Psi\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
be a path of interactions satisfying
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
.
If
\Psi
is
\beta
-invariant, then
\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}

belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.

Define
\Psi^{(0)}
as in (
5.55
) for our
\Psi
.
By Proposition
5.5
, we have

\left(\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\right)^{-1}\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}\in\mathop{\mathrm{HAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
On the other hand, applying Theorem
5.2
to
\Psi^{(0)}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
,
we see that
\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}
belongs to

\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}}).

Note that
\Psi^{(0)}(X;t)
is non-zero only if
X\subset H_{U}
or
X\subset H_{D}
,
and it coincides with
\Psi(X;t)
when it is non-zero.
Therefore, if
\Psi
is
\beta
-invariant,
\Psi^{(0)}
is
\beta_{g}^{U}
-invariant.
Therefore, by Theorem
5.2
, we have

\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi^{(0)}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GSQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Hence we have

\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}\in\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.

\square

Let
\Phi_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{UG}
be the fixed trivial interaction with a unique gapped ground state.
Its ground state
\omega_{0}:=\omega_{\Phi_{0}}
is of a product form (
2.21
).
For any
\Phi\in{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
, we have

\Phi_{0}\sim\Phi
.
Then by Theorem
5.1
,
there exists some

\Psi\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
with
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
for some
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
of the form

F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a
0<\theta<1
,
such that

\omega_{\Phi}=\omega_{\Phi_{0}}\circ\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}
.
From Theorem
5.2
,
\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}
belongs to

\mathop{\mathrm{SQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Because
\Phi\in{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}
,

\omega_{\Phi}=\omega_{\Phi_{0}}\circ\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}
is
\beta
-invariant.
Then, by Theorem
3.1
,

\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{\Phi})
is not empty.
Therefore, we may define
h_{\Phi}:=h(\omega_{\Phi})
by Definition
2.18
.

To see that
h_{\Phi}
is an invariant of
\sim_{\beta}
,
let
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{SL\beta}

with
\Phi_{1}\sim_{\beta}\Phi_{2}
.
Then by Theorem
5.1
,
there exists some
\beta
-invariant

\Psi\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
with
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])

for some
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
of the form

F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a constant
0<\theta<1

such that

\omega_{\Phi_{2}}=\omega_{\Phi_{1}}\circ\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}
.
Applying Theorem
5.6
,
to this
\Psi
,

\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi}

belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{GUQAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Then Theorem
4.1

implies

\displaystyle h_{\Phi_{2}}=h(\omega_{\Phi_{2}})=h(\omega_{\Phi_{1}}\circ\tau_{1,0}^{\Psi})=h(\omega_{\Phi_{1}})=h_{\Phi_{1}},

(5.60)

proving the stability.

\square

6
Automorphisms with factorized
d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha

When
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)
has some good factorization property with respect to
the action of
\beta_{g}^{U}
, the index
h(\omega)

can be calculated without going through GNS representations.

Definition 6.1
.

For
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)
, we set

\displaystyle\left(d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha\right)(g):=\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha\circ\left(\beta_{g}^{U}\right)^{-1},\quad g\in G.

(6.1)

We say that
d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha
is factorized into left and right if there are automorphisms

\gamma_{g,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}\right)
,
g\in G
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\left(d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha\right)(g)=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{g,L}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}\right),\quad g\in G.

(6.2)

For known examples of
2
-dimensional SPT-phases like

[
CGLW
]
and

[
MM
]

and
[
Y
]

or injective PEPS
[
MGSC
]
,
this property holds.
From such an automorphism, we can derive an outer action of
G
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)
be an automorphism.
Suppose that
d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha
is factorized into left and right i.e., there are automorphisms

\gamma_{g,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}\right)
,
g\in G
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle\left(d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha\right)(g)=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{g,L}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}\right),\quad g\in G.

(6.3)

Then there are unitaries
v_{\sigma}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}\right)
,

g,h\in G
,
\sigma=L,R
such that

\displaystyle\gamma_{g,{\sigma}}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\gamma_{h,{\sigma}}\beta_{h}^{{\sigma}U}\left(\gamma_{gh,{\sigma}}\beta_{gh}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right).

(6.4)

Because
\beta_{g}^{U}
is a group action, substituting (
6.3
), we get

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}}=\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha\circ\alpha^{-1}\beta_{h}^{U}\alpha\circ\left(\alpha^{-1}\beta_{gh}^{U}\alpha\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{g,L}\beta_{g}^{LU}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{h,L}\beta_{h}^{LU}\otimes\gamma_{h,R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\right)\circ\left(\gamma_{gh,L}\beta_{gh}^{LU}\otimes\gamma_{gh,R}\beta_{gh}^{RU}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{g,L}\beta_{g}^{LU}\gamma_{h,L}\beta_{h}^{LU}\left(\gamma_{gh,L}\beta_{gh}^{LU}\right)^{-1}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\gamma_{h,R}\beta_{h}^{RU}\left(\gamma_{gh,R}\beta_{gh}^{RU}\right)^{-1}\right)

(6.5)

By Lemma
B.1
, we then see that there are
unitaries
v_{\sigma}(g,h)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}\right)
,

g\in G
,
\sigma=L,R
satisfying (
6.4
).

\square

It is well known that
a third cohomology class can be associated to cocycle actions
[
C
]
[
J
]
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)
be an automorphism
such that
d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha
is factorized into left and right as (
6.3
).
Let

v_{\sigma}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}\right)
,

g,h\in G
,
\sigma=L,R

be unitaries satisfying (
6.4
), given in Lemma
6.2
.
Then
there is some
c_{{\sigma}}\in C^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
,
\sigma=L,R

such that

\displaystyle v_{{\sigma}}(g,h)v_{{\sigma}}(gh,k)=c_{{\sigma}}(g,h,k)\left(\gamma_{g,{\sigma}}\circ\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\left(v_{{\sigma}}(h,k)\right)\right)v_{{\sigma}}\left(g,hk\right),\quad g,h,k\in G.

(6.6)

By (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\hat{\gamma}_{h,\sigma}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\circ\hat{\gamma}_{gh,\sigma}

(6.7)

for

\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}:=\gamma_{g,{\sigma}}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}
.
Using this, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(gh,k)\right)\circ\hat{\gamma}_{ghk,\sigma}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\circ\hat{\gamma}_{gh,\sigma}\circ\hat{\gamma}_{k,\sigma}=\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\hat{\gamma}_{h,\sigma}\hat{\gamma}_{k,\sigma}=\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(h,k)\right)\circ\hat{\gamma}_{hk,\sigma}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\left(v_{\sigma}(h,k)\right)\right)\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\circ\hat{\gamma}_{hk,\sigma}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\left(v_{\sigma}(h,k)\right)v_{\sigma}(g,hk)\right)\circ\hat{\gamma}_{ghk,\sigma}.

(6.8)

Because
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{A}}=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
,

\hat{\gamma}_{g,\sigma}\left(v_{\sigma}(h,k)\right)v_{\sigma}(g,hk)

and
v_{\sigma}(g,h)v_{\sigma}(gh,k)
are proportional to each other, proving the Lemma.

\square

By the same argument as Lemma
2.4
, we can show that this
c_{R}
is actually a
3
-cocycle.
If
\omega\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
is given by
an automorphism
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega)

with factorized
d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha
, and if

\omega_{0}
is invariant under
\beta_{g}^{U}
,
then we have

h(\omega)=[c_{R}]_{H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})}
, for
c_{R}
given in Lemma
6.3
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
\omega_{0}
be a reference state of the form (
2.21
), and assume that

\omega_{0}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}=\omega_{0}
for any
g\in G
.
Let
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}\right)

be an automorphism.
Suppose that
d^{0}_{H_{U}}\alpha
is factorized into left and right as in (
6.3
)
with some
\gamma_{g,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{0}},\sigma}\right)

and
0<\theta_{0}<\frac{\pi}{2}
, for
\sigma=L,R
.
Let

v_{\sigma}(g,h)\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{H_{\sigma}}\right)
,

g,h\in G
,
\sigma=L,R

be unitaries satisfying (
6.4
) (given in Lemma
6.2
)
and
c_{R}\in C^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
satisfying (
6.6
)
for these
v_{R}(g,h)
(given in Lemma
6.3
).
Then we have
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
with
\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha)\neq\emptyset
,

c_{R}\in Z^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
,
and
h(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha)=[c_{R}]_{H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})}
.

That
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\in\mathop{\mathcal{SL}}\nolimits
is by definition.
Because

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)=\gamma_{g,{\sigma}}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\gamma_{h,{\sigma}}\beta_{h}^{{\sigma}U}\left(\gamma_{gh,{\sigma}}\beta_{gh}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{0}},\sigma}\right),

(6.9)

our
v_{\sigma}(g,h)
belongs to
{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{0}},\sigma}\right)
.
Because

\displaystyle\omega_{0}\alpha\circ\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha=\omega_{0}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha=\omega_{0}\alpha

(6.10)

and

\displaystyle\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha=({\rm{inner}})\circ\left(\gamma_{g,L}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{U},

(6.11)

with
\gamma_{g,\sigma}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{0},\sigma}}\right)
,
we have
(\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IG}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\alpha,\theta_{0})
,
and
(\gamma_{g,\sigma})\in{\mathcal{T}}\left(\theta_{0},\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha\right)
.
Clearly
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{EAut}}\nolimits(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha)
and there is
(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\alpha}^{\theta_{0}}
because
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{QAut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}})
.
Set
\gamma_{g}:=\gamma_{g,L}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}
.
From Lemma
2.1
, there is some
W_{g}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{0})

g\in G
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Theta\circ\gamma_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)^{-1},\quad g\in G.

(6.12)

In particular, because
v_{R}(h,k)
belongs to
{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta_{0}}\right)_{R}}\right)
,

\Theta\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\theta_{0}}^{c}}\right)
, and
\gamma_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}
preserves

{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(C_{\theta_{0}}\right)_{R}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{{L}}}\otimes\left(v_{R}(h,k)\right)\right)=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)\circ\Theta\circ\gamma_{g}\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{{L}}}\otimes\left(v_{R}(h,k)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\left(\alpha_{L}\otimes\alpha_{R}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}_{{L}}}\otimes\gamma_{g,R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{R}(h,k)\right)\right)=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\circ\gamma_{g,R}\beta_{g}^{RU}\left(v_{R}(h,k)\right)

(6.13)

On the other hand, (
6.4
) means

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)\right)\pi_{\sigma}=\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\circ\gamma_{g,{\sigma}}\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\gamma_{h,{\sigma}}\left(\beta_{g}^{{\sigma}U}\right)^{-1}\left(\gamma_{gh,{\sigma}}\right)^{-1}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}^{-1}.

(6.14)

From (
6.12
) and (
6.14
) we have

\displaystyle\left((W_{g}),(\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right))\right)\in\mathop{\mathrm{IP}}\nolimits\left(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha,\alpha,\theta_{0},(\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha),(\gamma_{g,\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta)\right).

(6.15)

Now from (
6.6
) and then (
6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\left(v_{R}(g,h)v_{R}(gh,k)\right)=c_{{R}}(g,h,k)\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\left(\left(\gamma_{g,{R}}\circ\beta_{g}^{{R}U}\left(v_{{R}}(h,k)\right)\right)v_{{R}}\left(g,hk\right)\right)

\displaystyle=c_{{R}}(g,h,k)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{g}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{H}}_{{L}}}\otimes\pi_{R}\alpha_{R}\left(v_{R}(h,k)\right)\right)\right)\cdot\left(\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{L}}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\alpha_{R}\left(v_{{R}}\left(g,hk\right)\right)\right).

(6.16)

This means

\displaystyle c_{R}=c_{R}\left(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha,\alpha,\theta_{0},(\alpha^{-1}\beta_{g}^{U}\alpha),(\gamma_{g,\sigma}),(\alpha_{L},\alpha_{R},\Theta),\left((W_{g}),((\pi_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma}\left(v_{\sigma}(g,h)\right)))\right)\right)

(6.17)

in the Definition
2.5
.
Hence we get

c_{R}\in Z^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})
,
and
h(\omega_{0}\circ\alpha)=[c_{R}]_{H^{3}(G,{\mathbb{T}})}
.

\square

Appendix A
Basic Notations

For a finite
set
S
,
\#S
indicates the number of elements in
S
.
For
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
[t]
denotes the smallest integer less than or equal to
t
.

For a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
,
B({\mathcal{H}})
denotes the set of all bounded operators on
{\mathcal{H}}
.
If
V:{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
is a linear map from a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
to
another Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
,
then
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V):B({\mathcal{H}}_{1})\to B({\mathcal{H}}_{2})
denotes the map

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V)(x):=VxV^{*}
,
x\in B({\mathcal{H}}_{1})
.
Occasionally we write
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{V}
instead of
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V)
.
For a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
and
v\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we set

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(v)(x):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v}(x):=vxv^{*}
,
x\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

For a state
\omega
,
\varphi
on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
,
we write
\omega\sim_{\rm q.e.}\varphi
when they are quasi-equivalent. (See
[
BR1
]
.)
We denote by
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits{\mathcal{B}}
the group of automorphisms on a
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
.
The group of inner automorphisms on a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
is denoted by
\mathop{\mathrm{Inn}}\nolimits{\mathcal{B}}
.
For
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})
,
\gamma_{1}=({\rm{inner}})\circ\gamma_{2}

means there is some unitary
u
in
{\mathcal{B}}
such that
\gamma_{1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(u)\circ\gamma_{2}
.
For a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
, the unit of
{\mathcal{B}}
is denoted by
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}}
.
For a Hilbert space we write
\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{H}}}:=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})}
.
For a unital
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}
, by
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{B}})
, we mean
the set of all unitary elements in
{\mathcal{B}}
.
For a Hilbert space we write
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}})
for
{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}))
.

For a state
\varphi
on
{\mathcal{B}}
and a
C^{*}
-subalgebra
{\mathcal{C}}
of
{\mathcal{B}}
,

\varphi|_{{\mathcal{C}}}
indicates the restriction of
\varphi
to
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Appendix B
Automorphisms on UHF-algebras

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
,
\mathfrak{B}
be UHF-algebras.
If automorphisms
\gamma_{\mathfrak{A}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{A})
,
\gamma_{\mathfrak{B}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\mathfrak{B})
and a unitary
W\in{\mathcal{U}}\left({\mathfrak{A}}\otimes\mathfrak{B}\right)

satisfy

\displaystyle\left(\gamma_{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes\gamma_{\mathfrak{B}}\right)(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{W}(X),\quad X\in{\mathfrak{A}\otimes\mathfrak{B}},

(B.1)

then there are unitaries
u_{\mathfrak{A}}\in{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{A})
and
u_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{B})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma_{\mathfrak{A}}\left(X_{\mathfrak{A}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{u_{\mathfrak{A}}}(X_{\mathfrak{A}}),\quad X_{\mathfrak{A}}\in{\mathfrak{A}},\\
&\gamma_{\mathfrak{B}}\left(X_{\mathfrak{B}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{u_{\mathfrak{B}}}(X_{\mathfrak{B}}),\quad X_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{\mathfrak{B}}.\end{split}

(B.2)

Fix some irreducible representations

({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{A}},\pi_{\mathfrak{A}})
,
({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}},\pi_{\mathfrak{B}})
,
of
\mathfrak{A}
,
\mathfrak{B}
.
We claim that there are unitaries
v_{\mathfrak{A}}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{A}})

and
v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}})
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}(X_{{\mathfrak{A}}})\right)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}(X_{{\mathfrak{A}}}),\quad X_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\in{\mathfrak{A}},\\
&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(X_{{\mathfrak{B}}})\right)=\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(X_{{\mathfrak{B}}}),\quad X_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in{\mathfrak{B}}.\end{split}

(B.3)

To see this, note that

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)(W)}\circ\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right).

(B.4)

From this,
\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
(resp.
\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
) is quasi-equivalent to

\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
(resp.
\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
).
Because
\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}
and
\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
are irreducible, by the Wigner theorem,
there are unitaries
v_{\mathfrak{A}}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{A}})

and
v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}})

satisfying (
B.3
).

We then have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)(W)}\circ\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)=\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\right)\otimes\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}}\circ\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\right)\otimes\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}\circ\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}\circ\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right).

(B.5)

Because
\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}
is irreducible, there is a
c\in{\mathbb{T}}

such that

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)(W)=c\left(v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right).

(B.6)

We claim there is a unitary
u_{\mathfrak{B}}\in{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{B})
such that

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(u_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)=v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}.

(B.7)

Choose a unit vector
\xi\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{A}}
with
\left\langle\xi,v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\xi\right\rangle\neq 0
.
For each
x\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}})
, the map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}}\ni(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\mapsto\left\langle\left(\xi\otimes\eta_{1}\right),x\left(\xi\otimes\eta_{2}\right)\right\rangle

(B.8)

is a bounded sesquilinear form.
Therefore, there is a unique
\Phi_{\xi}(x)\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}})
such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\eta_{1},\Phi_{\xi}(x)\eta_{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle\left(\xi\otimes\eta_{1}\right),x\left(\xi\otimes\eta_{2}\right)\right\rangle,\quad(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}}\times{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}}.

(B.9)

The map

\Phi_{\xi}:{\mathcal{B}}\left({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathfrak{B}})
is linear and

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi_{\xi}(x)\right\|\leq\left\|x\right\|,\quad x\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}).

(B.10)

Because
W
belongs to
\mathfrak{A}\otimes\mathfrak{B}
,
there are sequence

\displaystyle z_{N}=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{N}}a_{i}^{(N)}\otimes b_{i}^{(N)},\quad\text{with}\quad a_{i}^{(N)}\in{\mathfrak{A}},\quad b_{i}^{(N)}\in{\mathfrak{B}}

(B.11)

such that

\displaystyle\left\|W-z_{N}\right\|<\frac{1}{N}.

(B.12)

Because of (
B.10
),
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi_{\xi}\left(\left(\pi_{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\right)\left(W-z_{N}\right)\right)\right\|<\frac{1}{N}.

(B.13)

Note that

\displaystyle\Phi_{\xi}\left(\left(\pi_{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\right)\left(z_{N}\right)\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{N}}\left\langle\xi,\pi_{\mathfrak{A}}\left(a_{i}^{(N)}\right)\xi\right\rangle\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\left(b_{i}^{(N)}\right)\in\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathfrak{B}).

(B.14)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle c\left\langle\xi,v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\xi\right\rangle v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}=\Phi_{\xi}\left(c\left(v_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)\right)=\Phi_{\xi}\left(\left(\pi_{{\mathfrak{A}}}\otimes\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)(W)\right)\in\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}(\mathfrak{B})}^{n},

(B.15)

where
\overline{\cdot}^{n}
denotes the norm closure.
Because
\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\left(\mathfrak{B}\right)
is norm-closed, we have
\overline{\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\left(\mathfrak{B}\right)}^{n}=\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\left(\mathfrak{B}\right)
.
Hence we have
v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\in\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\left(\mathfrak{B}\right)
, i.e.,
there is a unitary
u_{\mathfrak{B}}\in\mathfrak{B}
such that
v_{{\mathfrak{B}}}=\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\left(u_{{\mathfrak{B}}}\right)
.

We then have

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{u_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}(u_{\mathfrak{B}})}\circ\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{v_{\mathfrak{B}}}\circ\pi_{{\mathfrak{B}}}(X)=\pi_{\mathfrak{B}}\circ\gamma_{\mathfrak{B}}(X),\quad X\in{\mathfrak{B}}.

(B.16)

As
{\mathfrak{B}}
is simple,

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits_{u_{{\mathfrak{B}}}}(X)=\gamma_{\mathfrak{B}}(X)

for all
X\in{\mathfrak{B}}
.

The proof for
{\mathfrak{A}}
is the same.

\square

Appendix C
F
-functions

In this section, we collect various estimates about
F
-functions.
Let us first start from the definition.

Definition C.1
.

An
F
-function
F
on
({{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},\mathrm{d})

is a non-increasing function
F:[0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)

such that

(i)

\left\|F\right\|:=\sup_{x\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\left(\sum_{y\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\right)<\infty
,
and

(ii)

C_{F}:=\sup_{x,y\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\left(\sum_{z\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\frac{F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,z)\right)F\left({\mathrm{d}}(z,y)\right)}{F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)}\right)<\infty
.

These are called
uniform integrability
and the
convolution identity
, respectively.

We denote by
{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
a class of
F
-functions which decay quickly.

Definition C.2
.

We say an
F
-function
F
belongs to
{\mathcal{F}}_{a}

if

(i)

for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
and
0<\theta\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{\theta,k,F}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)^{k}\left(F(n)\right)^{\theta}<\infty,

(C.1)

and

(ii)

for any
0<\theta<1
,
there is a
F
-function
\tilde{F}_{\theta}
such that

\displaystyle\max\left\{F\left(\frac{r}{3}\right),\left(F\left(\left[\frac{r}{3}\right]\right)\right)^{\theta}\right\}\leq\tilde{F}_{\theta}(r),\quad\quad r\geq 0.

(C.2)

For example, a function
F(r)=\frac{\exp\left({-r^{\theta}}\right)}{(1+r)^{4}}
with a constant
0<\theta<1

belongs to
{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
. (See section 8 of
[
NSY
]
.)

In this section, we derive inequalities about
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
.
In order for that the following Lemma is useful.
We will freely identify
{\mathbb{C}}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
in an obvious manner.

Lemma C.3
.

For
0\leq\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}\leq 2\pi
,
c>0
, and
r\geq 0
, set

\displaystyle S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}:=\left\{se^{i\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid r\leq s<r+c,\quad\theta\in[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]\right\}.

(C.3)

Then we
have

\displaystyle\#\left(S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+c\right)^{2}(r+1).

(C.4)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\#\left(S_{r,1}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)\leq 64(r+1).

(C.5)

Because the diameter of a
2
-dimensional unit square is
\sqrt{2}
,
any unit square
B
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
with
B\cap S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset

satisfies
B\subset\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\#\left\{B\mid\text{unit square of}\quad{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\quad\text{with }\quad B\cap S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\right\}=\sum_{B:B\cap S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset}1\leq\left|\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})\right|.

(C.6)

Note that the area of
\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})
, denoted by

\left|\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})\right|

is less than

\displaystyle\left|\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})\right|\leq\pi\left((r+c+\sqrt{2})^{2}-(r-\sqrt{2})^{2})\right)\leq\pi(2r+c)(2\sqrt{2}+c)\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+c\right)^{2}(r+1)

(C.7)

if
r>\sqrt{2}
.
For
r\leq\sqrt{2}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left|\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})\right|\leq\pi\left((r+c+\sqrt{2})^{2}\right)\leq\pi\cdot(2\sqrt{2}+c)^{2}\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+c\right)^{2}(r+1).

(C.8)

Hence in any case, we have

\displaystyle\left|\hat{S}_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}(\sqrt{2})\right|\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+c\right)^{2}(r+1).

(C.9)

Substituting this to (
C.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\#\left\{B\mid\text{unit square of}\quad{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\quad\text{with }\quad B\cap S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\right\}\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+c\right)^{2}(r+1).

(C.10)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\#\left\{S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right\}=\sum_{z\in S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}1=\sum_{z\in S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\sum_{B:\text{unit square of}\;{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{z\in B}

\displaystyle=\sum_{B:\text{unit square of}\;{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\sum_{z\in S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{z\in B}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}B:\\
\text{unit square of}\;{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\\
B\cap S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle=\#\left\{B\mid\text{unit square of}\quad{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\quad\text{with }\quad B\cap S_{r,c}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\right\}\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+c\right)^{2}(r+1).

(C.11)

\square

For an
F
-function
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
,
define a function
G_{F}
on
t\geq 0
by

\displaystyle G_{F}(t):=\sup_{x\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\left(\sum_{y\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},{\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\geq t}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\right),\quad t\geq 0.

(C.12)

Note that by uniform integrability the supremum is finite for all
t
.
In particular, for any
0<\theta<1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&G_{F}(t)\leq\sum_{r=[t]}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}:\\
r\leq\mathrm{d}(0,y)<r+1\end{subarray}}F\left(\mathrm{d}(0,y)\right)\leq\sum_{r=[t]}^{\infty}\sum_{y\in S_{r,1}^{[0,2\pi]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}F(r)\leq\sum_{r=[t]}^{\infty}\#\left(S_{r,1}^{[0,2\pi]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)F(r)\\
&\leq 64\sum_{r=[t]}^{\infty}(r+1)F(r)=64\sum_{r=[t]}^{\infty}(r+1)F(r)^{\theta}F(r)^{1-\theta}\leq 64\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}(r+1)F(r)^{\theta}\right)F([t])^{1-\theta}\leq 64\cdot\kappa_{\theta,1,F}\cdot F([t])^{1-\theta}<\infty.\end{split}

(C.13)

Substituting this, for any
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
0<\theta,\varphi<1
, and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(1+n)^{k}\left(G_{F}(n)\right)^{\alpha}\leq\left(64\cdot\kappa_{\theta,1,F}\right)^{\alpha}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(1+n)^{k}\cdot F(n)^{\alpha\left(1-\theta\right)}=\left(64\cdot\kappa_{\theta,1,F}\right)^{\alpha}\kappa_{\alpha\left(1-\theta\right),k,F}<\infty,\\
&\sum_{n=[\frac{r}{3}]}^{\infty}(1+n)^{k}\left(G_{F}(n)\right)^{\alpha}\leq\left(64\cdot\kappa_{\theta,1,F}\right)^{\alpha}\sum_{n=[\frac{r}{3}]}^{\infty}(1+n)^{k}\cdot\left(F(n)^{\alpha\left(1-\theta\right)}\right)^{(1-\varphi)}\left(F(n)^{\alpha\left(1-\theta\right)}\right)^{\varphi}\\
&=\left(64\cdot\kappa_{\theta,1,F}\right)^{\alpha}\kappa_{\alpha\left(1-\theta\right)\left(1-\varphi\right),k,F}F\left(\left[\frac{r}{3}\right]\right)^{\alpha\left(1-\theta\right)\varphi}.\end{split}

(C.14)

For any
0<c\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}F(cr)(r+2)^{3}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\\
l\leq cr<l+1\end{subarray}}F(cr)(r+2)^{3}\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\\
\frac{l}{c}\leq r<\frac{l+1}{c}\end{subarray}}F(l)\left(\frac{l+1}{c}+2\right)^{3}\\
&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}F(l)\left(\frac{l+1}{c}+2\right)^{3}\left(\frac{l+1}{c}-(\frac{l}{c}-1)+1\right)\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}F(l)\left(\frac{l+1}{c}+2\right)^{4}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{c^{4}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}F(l)\left(l+3\right)^{4}\leq\frac{3^{4}\kappa_{1,4,F}}{c^{4}}<\infty.\end{split}

(C.15)

We also have for
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
0<c\leq 1
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{r_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}c\geq(m+1)\end{subarray}}(r_{1}+1)F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}c-(m+1)\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\\
l\leq\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}c-(m+1)<l+1\end{subarray}}(r_{1}+1)F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}c-(m+1)\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\\
l\leq\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}c-(m+1)<l+1\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{l+m+2}{c}+1\right)\cdot F(l)\\
&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\#\left\{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\cap S_{\frac{l+m+1}{c},\frac{1}{c}}^{[0,2\pi]}\right\}\left(\frac{l+m+2}{c}+1\right)\cdot F(l)\\
&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{l+m+1}{c}+1\right)\cdot\left(\frac{l+m+2}{c}+1\right)\cdot F(l)\\
&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{l+m+3}{c}\right)^{2}\cdot F(l)\\
&\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\frac{(m+3)^{2}}{c^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(l+1\right)^{2}F(l)\\
&\leq\pi\left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\frac{(m+3)^{2}}{c^{2}}\kappa_{1,2,F}\leq\left(\frac{3}{c}\right)^{2}\left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\pi{(m+1)^{2}}\kappa_{1,2,F}.\end{split}

(C.16)

Recall (
5.27
) and (
5.28
).

Lemma C.4
.

Let
\varphi_{1}<\varphi_{2}<\varphi_{3}<\varphi_{4}
with
\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{1}<2\pi
.
Then
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]},\\
y\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\end{subarray}}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\leq(64)^{3}\frac{3^{4}\kappa_{1,4,F}}{\left(c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}\right)^{4}}.

(C.17)

Let
x=s_{1}e^{i\phi_{1}}\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}

and
y=s_{2}e^{i\phi_{2}}\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}
with
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0
.
If
\cos\left(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}\right)\geq 0
, then
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{d}}(x,y)=\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}-2s_{1}s_{2}\cos\left(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}\right)}\geq\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}}\sqrt{1-\cos\left(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}\right)}\\
&\geq\sqrt{1-\max\left\{\cos\left(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{2}\right),\cos\left(\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{1}\right),0\right\}}\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}}.\end{split}

(C.18)

If
\cos\left(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}\right)<0
, then
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{d}}(x,y)=\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}-2s_{1}s_{2}\cos\left(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}\right)}\geq\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}}.\\
\end{split}

(C.19)

Hence for any
x=s_{1}e^{i\phi_{1}}\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}

and
y=s_{2}e^{i\phi_{2}}\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}
with
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0

we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\geq\sqrt{1-\max\left\{\cos\left(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{2}\right),\cos\left(\varphi_{4}-\varphi_{1}\right),0\right\}}\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}}=c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}\sqrt{s_{1}^{2}+s_{2}^{2}}.

(C.20)

Substituting this estimate, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]},\\
y\in\check{C}_{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\end{subarray}}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{r_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r_{2}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in S_{r_{1},1}^{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\\
y\in S_{r_{2},1}^{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\end{subarray}}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{r_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r_{2}=0}^{\infty}F\left({c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}}\sqrt{r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}})\right)\#\left(S_{r_{1},1}^{[\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)\#\left(S_{r_{2},1}^{[\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq(64)^{2}\sum_{r_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{r_{2}=0}^{\infty}F\left({c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}}\sqrt{r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}})\right)(r_{1}+1)(r_{2}+1)\\
&\leq(64)^{2}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1},r_{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\\
(r_{1},r_{2})\in S_{r,1}^{[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\end{subarray}}F\left({c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}}\sqrt{r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}})\right)(r_{1}+1)(r_{2}+1)\\
&\leq(64)^{2}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}F\left({c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}}r\right)(r+2)^{2}\cdot\#\left(S_{r}^{[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)\\
&\leq(64)^{3}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}F\left({c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}}r\right)(r+2)^{3}\\
&\leq(64)^{3}\frac{3^{4}\kappa_{1,4,F}}{\left(c^{(0)}_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3},\varphi_{4}}\right)^{4}}\end{split}

(C.21)

We used Lemma
C.3
to bound
\#\left(S_{r,1}^{[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)
etc.
At the last inequality we used (
C.15
)

\square

Set

\displaystyle L_{\varphi}:=\left\{z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid\arg z=\varphi\right\},\quad\varphi\in[0,2\pi).

(C.22)

and

\displaystyle{c^{(1)}}_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3}}:=\sqrt{1-\max\left\{\cos(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2}),\cos(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{3})\right\}},\quad\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2},\zeta_{3}\in[0,2\pi).

(C.23)

Lemma C.5
.

Let
\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}

with
\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}
and

0<\left|\varphi-\theta_{0}\right|<\frac{\pi}{2}

for all
\theta_{0}\in[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in\check{C}_{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}}\sum_{y\in L_{\varphi}(m)}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\leq 64\cdot 144\cdot 24\cdot\left({c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\right)^{-4}\left(\pi\kappa_{1,2,F}+F(0)\right)(m+1)^{4},

(C.24)

for any
m\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}

For each
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, set

\displaystyle T_{\varphi,r,m}:=\left\{se^{i\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid r\leq s\cos(\theta-\varphi)\leq r+1,\quad-m\leq s\sin(\theta-\varphi)\leq m\right\}.

(C.25)

Note that
s\cos(\theta-\varphi)
is a projection of
se^{i\theta}
onto
L_{\varphi}

and
|s\sin(\theta-\varphi)|
is the distance of
se^{i\theta}
from the line including

L_{\varphi}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle L_{\varphi}(m)\subset\cup_{r=-m}^{\infty}T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\hat{T}_{\varphi,r,m}(\sqrt{2})\right|\leq(2\sqrt{2}+1)(2m+2\sqrt{2})\leq 12(m+1).

(C.26)

Because the diameter of a
2
-dimensional unit square is
\sqrt{2}
,
any unit square
B
of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
with
B\cap T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset

satisfies
B\subset\hat{T}_{\varphi,r,m}(\sqrt{2})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\#\left\{B\mid\text{unit square of}\quad{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\quad\text{with }\quad B\cap T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\right\}=\sum_{B:B\cap T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset}1\\
&\leq\left|\hat{T}_{\varphi,r,m}(\sqrt{2})\right|\leq 12(m+1).\end{split}

(C.27)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\#\left\{T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right\}=\sum_{z\in T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}1=\sum_{z\in T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\sum_{B:\text{unit square of}\;{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{z\in B}

\displaystyle=\sum_{B:\text{unit square of}\;{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\sum_{z\in T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{I}_{z\in B}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}B:\\
\text{unit square of}\;{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\\
B\cap T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle=\#\left\{B\mid\text{unit square of}\quad{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\quad\text{with }\quad B\cap T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\neq\emptyset\right\}\leq 12(m+1).

(C.28)

If
x\in\check{C}_{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}
, we have
x=r_{0}e^{i\theta_{0}}

for some
r_{0}\geq 0
and
\theta_{0}\in[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]
.
By the assumption, we have
0<|\theta_{0}-\varphi|<\frac{\pi}{2}

hence
0<\cos(\varphi-\theta_{0})<1
.
Therefore, for any
r\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathrm{d}(x,re^{i\varphi})=\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}-2r_{0}r\cos(\theta_{0}-\varphi)}\geq\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}\sqrt{1-\cos(\theta_{0}-\varphi)}\\
&\geq\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}\sqrt{1-\max\left\{\cos(\theta_{1}-\varphi),\cos(\theta_{2}-\varphi)\right\}}.\end{split}

(C.29)

Therefore, for
any
x\in\check{C}_{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}
and
y\in T_{\varphi,r,m}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\geq\mathrm{d}(x,re^{i\varphi})-(m+1)=\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}-(m+1).\end{split}

(C.30)

From this and (
C.26
) and (
C
), for any

x=r_{0}e^{i\theta_{0}}\in C_{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}
,
r_{0}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{y\in L_{\varphi}(m)}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\leq\sum_{r=-m}^{\infty}\sum_{y\in\left(T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\leq\sum_{r=-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{y\in\left(T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathbb{Z}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\geq(m+1)\end{subarray}}\sum_{y\in\left(T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)}F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}-(m+1)\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathbb{Z}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}<(m+1)\end{subarray}}\sum_{y\in\left(T_{\varphi,r,m}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\right)}F\left(0\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathbb{Z}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\geq(m+1)\end{subarray}}12(m+1)F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}-(m+1)\right)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathbb{Z}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}<(m+1)\end{subarray}}12(m+1)F\left(0\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\geq(m+1)\end{subarray}}24(m+1)F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{0}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}-(m+1)\right)\\
&+36\frac{(m+1)^{2}}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}F(0)\mathbb{I}_{r_{0}\leq\frac{m+1}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}}\end{split}

(C.31)

We then get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{x\in\check{C}_{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}}\sum_{y\in L_{\varphi}(m)}F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{r_{1}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{x\in S_{r_{1},1}^{[\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\\
&\left(\begin{gathered}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\geq(m+1)\end{subarray}}24(m+1)F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}-(m+1)\right)\\
+36\frac{(m+1)^{2}}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}F(0)\mathbb{I}_{r_{1}\leq\frac{m+1}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}}\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{r_{1}=0}^{\infty}64(r_{1}+1)\\
&\left(\begin{gathered}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}:\\
\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\geq(m+1)\end{subarray}}24(m+1)F\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+r_{1}^{2}}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}-(m+1)\right)\\
+36\frac{(m+1)^{2}}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}F(0)\mathbb{I}_{r_{1}\leq\frac{m+1}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}}\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\leq 64\cdot 24\cdot\left(\frac{3}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\right)^{2}\left(2\sqrt{2}+\frac{1}{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\right)^{2}\pi{(m+1)^{3}}\kappa_{1,2,F}+64\cdot 36\cdot\frac{(m+1)^{2}}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}F(0)\left(\frac{m+1}{{c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}}+1\right)^{2}\\
&\leq 64\cdot 144\cdot 24\cdot\left({c^{(1)}}_{\varphi,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}\right)^{-4}\left(\pi\kappa_{1,2,F}+F(0)\right)(m+1)^{4}.\end{split}

(C.32)

We used (
C.16
).

\square

Appendix D
Quasi-local automorphisms

In this section we collect some results from
[
NSY
]
, and prove Theorem
5.1
.

Definition D.1
.

A norm-continuous interaction on
{\mathcal{A}}
defined on an interval
[0,1]

is a map

\Phi:{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\times[0,1]\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}
such that

(i)

for any
t\in[0,1]
,
\Phi(\cdot,t):{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}

is an interaction, and

(ii)

for any
Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
, the map

\Phi(Z,\cdot):[0,1]\to{\mathcal{A}}_{Z}

is norm-continuous.

To ensure that the interactions induce quasi-local automorphisms we need to impose sufficient decay properties on the interaction strength.

Definition D.2
.

Let
F
be an
F
-function on
({{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},\mathrm{d})
.
We denote by
{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
the set of all
norm continuous interactions
\Phi
on
{\mathcal{A}}
defined on an interval
[0,1]

such that the function
\left\|\Phi\right\|_{F}:[0,1]\to{\mathbb{R}}
defined by

\displaystyle\left\|\Phi\right\|_{F}(t):=\sup_{x,y\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\frac{1}{F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)}\sum_{Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},Z\ni x,y}\left\|\Phi(Z;t)\right\|,\quad t\in[0,1],

(D.1)

is uniformly bounded, i.e.,

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Phi\right\|(t)<\infty
.
It follows that
t\mapsto\left\|\Phi\right\|_{F}(t)
is integrable, and we set

\displaystyle I_{F}(\Phi):=I_{1,0}(\Phi):=C_{F}\int_{0}^{1}dt\left\|\Phi\right\|_{F}(t),

(D.2)

with
C_{F}
given in Definition
C.1
.
We also set

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\Phi\right\|\right|_{F}:=\sup_{x,y\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\frac{1}{F\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)}\sum_{Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},Z\ni x,y}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left(\left\|\Phi(Z;t)\right\|\right)

(D.3)

and denote by
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
the set of all
\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])

with
\left\|\left|\Phi\right\|\right|<\infty
.

We will need some more notation.
For
\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
and
0\leq m\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
we introduce a path of interactions
\Phi_{m}
by

\displaystyle\Phi_{m}\left(X;t\right):=|X|^{m}\Phi\left(X;t\right),\quad X\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2}),\quad t\in[0,1].

(D.4)

An interaction gives rise to local (and here, time-dependent) Hamiltonians, via

\displaystyle H_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t):=\sum_{Z\in\Lambda}\Phi(Z,t),\quad t\in[0,1],\quad\Lambda\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}.

(D.5)

We denote by
U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)
, the solution of

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)=-iH_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t)U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s),\quad s,t\in[0,1]

(D.6)

\displaystyle U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(s;s)=\mathbb{I}.

(D.7)

We define corresponding automorphisms
\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi},\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}
on
{\mathcal{A}}
by

\displaystyle\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}(A):=U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)^{*}AU_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s),

(D.8)

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}(A):=U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)AU_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)^{*},

(D.9)

with
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}={\tau}_{s,t}^{(\Lambda),\Phi},

(D.10)

by the uniqueness of the solution of the differential equation.

Theorem D.3

(
[
NSY
]
)
.

Let
F
be an
F
-function on
({{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},\mathrm{d})
.
Suppose that
\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
.
Then the following holds:

1.

The limits

\displaystyle\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}(A):=\lim_{\Lambda\nearrow{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}(A),\quad\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{\Phi}(A):=\lim_{\Lambda\nearrow{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}(A),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}},\quad t,s\in[0,1]

(D.11)

exist and defines strongly continuous families of automorphisms on
{\mathcal{A}}

such that
\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{\Phi}=\tau_{s,t}^{\Phi}={\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}}^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{t,s}^{\Phi}\circ\hat{\tau}_{s,u}^{\Phi}=\hat{\tau}_{t,u}^{\Phi},\quad\tau_{t,t}^{\Phi}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathcal{A}}},\quad t,s,u\in[0,1].

(D.12)

2.

For any
X,Y\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
with
X\cap Y=\emptyset

the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}(A),B\right]\right\|\leq\frac{2\left\|A\right\|\left\|B\right\|}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Phi)}-1\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(d(X,Y)\right)

(D.13)

holds for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
,
B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{Y}
, and
t,s\in[0,1]
.

If
\Lambda\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
and
X\cup Y\subset\Lambda
, a similar bound holds for
\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}
.

3.

For any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}(A)\right)\right\|\leq\frac{8\left\|A\right\|}{C_{F}}\left(e^{2I_{F}(\Phi)}-1\right)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(m\right),

(D.14)

for

A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
.
Here we set
\Delta_{X(0)}:=\Pi_{X}
and
\Delta_{X(m)}:=\Pi_{X(m)}-\Pi_{X(m-1)}

for
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
A similar bound holds for
\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}
.
(See (
C.12
) for the definition of
G_{F}
.)

4.

For any
X,\Lambda\in{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{2})
with
X\subset\Lambda
, and
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}(A)-\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}(A)\right\|\leq\frac{2}{C_{F}}\left\|A\right\|e^{2I_{F}(\Phi)}I_{F}(\Phi)\left|X\right|G_{F}\left(d\left(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\setminus\Lambda\right)\right).

(D.15)

5.

If
\beta_{g}^{U}\left(\Phi(X;t)\right)=\Phi(X;t)
for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
, and

g\in G
, then
we have

\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}=\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}

for any
t,s\in[0,1]
and

g\in G
,

Item
1
is Theorem 3.5 of
[
NSY
]
, while
2
and
4
follow from Corollary 3.6 of the same paper by a straightforward bounding of
D(X,Y)
and the summation in eq. (3.80) of
[
NSY
]
respectively.
Item
3
can be obtained using
2
and
[
NSY
, Cor. 4.4]
.

Suppose that
{\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(\Phi(X;t)\right)=\Phi(X;t)
for any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,
t\in[0,1]
, and

g\in G
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)\right)=-{\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(iH_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t)\right){\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)\right)=-iH_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t){\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)\right),\quad t\in[0,1]

(D.16)

and
{\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(s;s)\right)=\mathbb{I}
.
Hence
{\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)\right)
and
U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)

satisfy the same differential equation and initial condition.
Therefore we get
{\beta_{g}^{U}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)\right)=U_{\Lambda,\Phi}(t;s)
.
From this, we obtain
{\beta_{g}^{U}}\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}=\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda),\Phi}{\beta_{g}^{U}}
,
and taking
\Lambda\uparrow{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we obtain

{\beta_{g}^{U}}\circ\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}=\tau_{t,s}^{\Phi}\circ{\beta_{g}^{U}}
.

\square

The following is slightly strengthened version of Assumption 5.15. of
[
NSY
]
.

Assumption D.4
.

[
[
NSY
]
]
We assume that the family of linear maps

\{{\mathcal{K}}_{t}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}\to{\mathcal{A}}\}_{t\in[0,1]}

is norm
continuous and satisfy the followings: There is
a family of linear maps

\{{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\}_{t\in[0,1]}

for each
n\geq 1
such that:

(i)

For each
n\geq 1
, the family

\{{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\}_{t\in[0,1]}

satisfies

(a)

For each
t\in[0,1]
,
\left({\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}(A)\right)^{*}={\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}(A^{*})

for all
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}
.

(b)

For each
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}
, the function
[0,1]\ni t\to{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}(A)
is norm continuous.

(c)

For each
t\in[0,1]
the map
{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}:{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{n}}

is norm
continuous and moreover, this
continuity is uniform on
[0,1]
.

(ii)

There is some
p\geq 0
and a constant
B_{1}>0
for which
given any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
and
n\geq 1
large enough so that

X\subset\Lambda_{n}

\left\|{\mathcal{K}}^{(n)}_{t}(A)\right\|\leq B_{1}|X|^{p}\left\|A\right\|,\quad\text{for all}\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}\quad\text{and}\quad t\in[0,1].

(iii)

There is some
q\geq 0
, a non-negative, non-increasing function
G
with

G(x)\to 0
as
x\to\infty
,
and a constant
C_{1}>0

for which given any sets
X,Y\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

and
n\geq 1

large enough so that
X\cup Y\subset\Lambda_{n}
,

\left\|\left[{\mathcal{K}}^{(n)}_{t}(A),B\right]\right\|\leq C_{1}|X|^{q}\left\|A\right\|\left\|B\right\|G\left(\mathrm{d}(X,Y)\right),\quad\text{for all}\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X},\quad B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{Y}\quad\text{and}\quad t\in[0,1].

(iv)

There is some
r\geq 0
, a non-negative, non-increasing function
H

with
H(x)\to 0
as
x\to\infty
,
and a constant
D_{1}>0

for which given any
X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

there exists
N\geq 1
such that for
n\geq N

\left\|{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}(A)-{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(A)\right\|\leq D_{1}|X|^{r}\left\|A\right\|H\left(\mathrm{d}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\setminus\Lambda_{n})\right)

for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
and
t\in[0,1]
.

The following theorem is a slight modification of Theorem 5.17 of
[
NSY
]

Theorem D.5
.

Let
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
, with
\tilde{F}_{\theta}
in (
C.2
) for each
0<\theta<1
.
Assume
that
\{{\mathcal{K}}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}

is a family of linear maps satisfying Assumption
D.4
,
with
G=G_{F}
in (iii).
(Recall Definition
C.2
and (
C.12
)).
Let

\Phi\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])

be an interaction satisfying
\Phi_{m}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
for
m=\max\{p,q,r\}

where

p,q,r
are numbers in Assumption
D.4
.
Then,
the right hand side of the following sum

\displaystyle\Psi\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,\;X(m)=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left({\mathcal{K}}_{t}\left(\Phi\left(X;t\right)\right)\right),\quad Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},\quad t\in[0,1]

(D.17)

defines a path of interaction such that
\Psi\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}_{\theta}}([0,1])
, for any
0<\theta<1
.
Furthermore, the formula

\displaystyle\Psi^{(n)}\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,X(m)\cap\Lambda_{n}=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left({\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}\left(\Phi\left(X;t\right)\right)\right)

(D.18)

defines
\Psi^{{(n)}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}_{\theta}}([0,1])
, for any
0<\theta<1
such that

\Psi^{(n)}\left(Z,t\right)=0
unless
Z\subset\Lambda_{n}
,
and satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{t}^{(n)}\left(H_{\Lambda_{n},\Phi}(t)\right)=H_{\Lambda_{n},\Psi^{(n)}}(t).

(D.19)

For any
t,u\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\tau_{t,u}^{\Psi^{(n)}}\left(A\right)-\tau_{t,u}^{\Psi}\left(A\right)\right\|=0,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

(D.20)

Furthermore, if
\Phi_{m+k}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
, then we have

\Psi_{k}^{{(n)}},\Psi_{k}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}_{\theta}}([0,1])

for ant
0<\theta<1
.

Because of
F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
, we see from
(
C.14
) that for any

and
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

has a finite

-moment.
We also recall (

) and (

) to see that

\displaystyle\max\left\{F\left(\frac{r}{3}\right),\sum_{n=[\frac{r}{3}]}^{\infty}(1+n)^{5}G_{F}(n)^{\alpha}\right\}\leq\tilde{C}\tilde{F}_{\alpha(1-\theta^{\prime})\varphi}(r),\quad\quad r\geq 0,

(D.21)

for any
0<\alpha,\theta^{\prime},\varphi<1
.
As this holds for any
0<\alpha,\theta^{\prime},\varphi<1
, the condition in (ii) of Theorem 5.17
[
NSY
]

holds for any
\tilde{F}_{\theta}
.
Therefore, from (ii) of Theorem 5.17
[
NSY
]
, we get

\Psi,\Psi^{(n)}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}_{\theta}}\left([0,1]\right)

and

\Psi^{(n)}
converges locally in

-norm to
\Psi
with respect to
\tilde{F}_{\theta}
,
for any
0<\theta<1
.

Theorem 5.13 of
[
NSY
]
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\left\|\Psi^{(n)}\right\|_{\tilde{F}_{\theta}}(t)dt<\infty,

(D.22)

see also
[
NSY
, eq. (5.101)]
.
Therefore, from Theorem 3.8 of
[
NSY
]
, we obtain (
D.20
).

By the proof of Theorem 5.17 and Theorem 5.13 (5.87) of
[
NSY
]
,
if
\Phi_{k+m}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
for some

, then we have

\Psi_{k}^{{(n)(s)}},\Psi_{k}^{{(s)}}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
.
More precisely, instead of (5.88) of
[
NSY
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\\
Z\ni x,y\end{subarray}}|Z|^{k}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Psi(Z;t)\right\|\\
&\leq B_{1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\\
Z\ni x,y\end{subarray}}|Z|^{k+p}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Phi(Z;t)\right\|+4C_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}G_{F}(n)\sum_{X:X(n+1)\ni x,y}|X|^{q}|X(n+1)|^{k}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Phi(X;t)\right\|\\
&\leq B_{1}\left\|\left|\Phi_{k+p}\right|\right\|_{F}F\left(\mathrm{d}(x,y)\right)+4C_{1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}G_{F}(n)(2n+3)^{2k}\sum_{X:X(n+1)\ni x,y}|X|^{q+k}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\|\Phi(X;t)\right\|\\
&\leq B_{1}\left\|\left|\Phi_{k+p}\right|\right\|_{F}F\left(\mathrm{d}(x,y)\right)+\tilde{C}_{\theta}\tilde{F}_{\theta}\left(\mathrm{d}(x,y)\right)\left\|\left|\Phi_{q+k}\right|\right\|_{F}<\infty\end{split}

(D.23)

with some constant
\tilde{C}_{\theta}
, for each
0<\theta<1
.
In the last line we used (
C.14
) and Lemma 8.9 of
[
NSY
]
.
Hence we get
\Psi_{k}^{{(n)}},\Psi_{k}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}_{\theta}}([0,1])
.

\square

Suppose
\Phi_{0}\sim\Phi_{1}
via a path
\Phi
.
Our definition of
\Phi_{0}\sim\Phi_{1}
means the existence of
a path of interactions satisfying Assumption 1.2 of
[

]
.
Therefore, Theorem 1.3 of
[
MO
]

guarantees the existence of a path of quasi-local automorphism
\alpha_{t}

satisfying

\omega_{\Phi_{1}}=\omega_{\Phi_{0}}\circ\alpha_{1}
.
From the proof in

]
,
the automorphism
\alpha_{t}
is given by a family of interactions

\displaystyle\Psi\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,\;X(m)=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left({\mathcal{K}}_{t}\left(\dot{\Phi}\left(X;t\right)\right)\right),\quad Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},\quad t\in[0,1],

(D.24)

with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{t}(A):=-\int duW_{\gamma}(u)\tau_{u}^{\Phi(t)}(A),

(D.25)

as
\alpha_{t}=\tau_{t,0}^{\Psi}
.
(Note that by partial integral of (1.19) of
[
MO
]
, we obtain (6.103) of
[
NSY
]
with function

W_{\gamma}
in (6.35) of
[
NSY
]
).)
The interaction
\Psi
actually belongs to
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F_{3}}([0,1])

for some
F_{3}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
.
To see this, note that the path
\Phi
in Definition
1.2

satisfy Assumption 6.12 of
[
NSY
]

for any
F
-function because

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\\
X\ni x,y\end{subarray}}\left(\left\|\Phi\left(X;s\right)\right\|+|X|\left\|\dot{\Phi}\left(X;s\right)\right\|\right)\leq\frac{2^{(2R+1)^{2}}C_{b}^{\Phi}}{F(R)}F(\mathrm{d}(x,y)),

(D.26)

with
C_{b}^{\Phi}
,
R
, given in
3, 4
of Definition
1.2
.
In particular, it satisfies Assumption 6.12 of
[
NSY
]
, with
respect to the

-function (see section 8 of
[
NSY
]
)

F_{1}(r):=\frac{e^{-r}}{(1+r)^{4}}
. By section 8 of
[

]

belongs to
{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
.
Fix any

.
Then by Proposition 6.13 and its proof of
[

]
, the family of maps given by (

)
((6.102) of
[
NSY
]
) satisfies Assumption

, with

,

,

and

, where

.
Furthermore, we have

for any

,
because

\displaystyle\left\|\left|\dot{\Phi}_{m}\right\|\right|_{F_{2}}:=\sup_{x,y\in{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}}\frac{1}{F_{2}\left({\mathrm{d}}(x,y)\right)}\sum_{Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},Z\ni x,y}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left|Z\right|^{m}\left(\left\|\dot{\Phi}(Z;t)\right\|\right)\leq\frac{2^{(2R+1)^{2}}(2R+1)^{2m}C_{b}^{\Phi}}{F_{2}(R)}<\infty.

(D.27)

We have
F_{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
, and fixing any

,

\tilde{F}_{2}(r):=(1+r)^{-4}\exp\left(-r^{\alpha^{\prime}}\right)

satisfy

\displaystyle\max\left\{F_{2}\left(\frac{r}{3}\right),\left(F_{2}\left(\left[\frac{r}{3}\right]\right)\right)^{\theta}\right\}\leq C_{2,\theta,\alpha^{\prime}}\tilde{F}_{2}(r),\quad\quad r\geq 0,

(D.28)

for a suitable constant

.

Therefore, by Theorem
D.5
,

\Psi
given by (
D.24
) for this
{\mathcal{K}}_{t}
and
\dot{\Phi}

for
\tilde{F}_{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
above.

If
\Phi
is
\beta_{g}
-invariant, then
\tau^{\Phi(t)}
commutes with

\beta_{g}
, hence
{\mathcal{K}}_{t}
commutes with
\beta_{g}
.
As
\Pi_{X}
commutes with
\beta_{g}
and
\dot{\Phi}
is
\beta_{g}
-invariant, we see that

\Psi
is
\beta_{g}
-invariant.

\square

Proposition D.6
.

Let
F,\tilde{F}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{a}
be
F
-functions
of the form

F(r)=(1+r)^{-4}\exp\left(-r^{\theta}\right)
,

\tilde{F}(r):=(1+r)^{-4}\exp\left(-r^{\theta^{\prime}}\right)

with some constants
0<\theta^{\prime}<\theta<1
.
Let
\Psi,\tilde{\Psi}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
be a path of interactions such that

\Psi_{1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
.
Finally, let
\tau_{t,s}^{\tilde{\Psi}}
and
\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),{\tilde{\Psi}}}
be
automorphisms given by
\Psi,\tilde{\Psi}
from Theorem
D.3
.

Then, with
s\in[0,1]
,
the right hand side of the following sum

\displaystyle\Xi^{(s)}\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,\;X(m)=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,s}^{\tilde{\Psi}}\left(\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right)\right),\quad Z\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}},\quad t\in[0,1]

(D.29)

defines a path of interaction such that
\Xi^{(s)}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
.
Furthermore, the formula

\displaystyle\Xi^{(n)(s)}\left(Z,t\right):=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{X\subset Z,X(m)\cap\Lambda_{n}=Z}\Delta_{X(m)}\left(\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Psi}}\left(\Psi\left(X;t\right)\right)\right)

(D.30)

defines
\Xi^{{(n)(s)}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
such that

\Xi^{(n)}\left(Z,t\right)=0
unless
Z\subset\Lambda_{n}
,
and satisfies

\displaystyle\tau_{t,s}^{(\Lambda_{n}),\tilde{\Psi}}\left(H_{\Lambda_{n},\Psi}(t)\right)=H_{\Lambda_{n},\Xi^{(n)(s)}}(t).

(D.31)

For any
t,u\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\|\tau_{t,u}^{\Xi^{(n)(s)}}\left(A\right)-\tau_{t,u}^{\Xi^{(s)}}\left(A\right)\right\|=0,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}.

(D.32)

Furthermore, if
\Psi_{1}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{F}([0,1])
, then we have

\Xi^{{(n)(s)}},\Xi^{{(s)}}\in\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\tilde{F}}([0,1])
.

From Theorem
D.5
, it suffices to show that
the family
\{{\mathcal{K}}_{t}:=\tau_{t,u}^{\tilde{\Psi}}\}

satisfies the Assumption
D.4
.
This follows from Theorem
D.3
.

\square
